- Consolidated data - All Vulnerable sections in India -
Vulnerable Section Key Problems Faced Solutions / Government Schemes Recent Statistics (2023-2025)
Women (General & Marginalized) - Gender-based violence (domestic, sexual harassment, rape).
- Economic inequality, unpaid care work, job loss post-COVID.
- Discrimination in education, healthcare; multiple vulnerabilities for Dalit/Adivasi women.
- Declining child sex ratio, child marriage. 		- Protection of Women from Domestic Violence Act (PWDVA), 2005: Civil remedies, protection orders.
- Sexual Harassment at Workplace (POSH) Act, 2013: Internal Complaints Committees.
- Beti Bachao Beti Padhao: Promotes girl child education, gender equality.
- One-Stop Centres (802 operational in 2025): Integrated support for violence survivors.
- Pradhan Mantri Matru Vandana Yojana: Maternity benefits. 		- 4% rise in crimes against women (NCRB 2023); 4.45 lakh cases annually (2024).
- Gender Gap Index: India ranked 131/148 (2025).
- Enabling women participation could boost GDP by 16% by 2025.
Children (Especially Girls & Marginalized) - Child labor, trafficking, sexual abuse under POCSO.
- Malnutrition, lack of education, gender-biased sex selection.
- Vulnerability to diseases, exploitation in urban slums.
- Impact of COVID on learning outcomes. 		- Protection of Children from Sexual Offences (POCSO) Act, 2012: Child-friendly procedures.
- Integrated Child Development Services (ICDS): Nutrition, health for 0-6 years.
- Childline 1098: 24/7 emergency support.
- Juvenile Justice Act, 2015: Care and protection.
- Samagra Shiksha: Universal education access. 		- 9.2% rise in crimes against children (NCRB 2023); significant POCSO cases.
- 47,000 trafficking cases (2024 NCRB, overlapping with women).
- National Girl Child Day (Jan 24) highlights education challenges.
Scheduled Castes (Dalits) - Caste-based discrimination, violence, untouchability.
- Economic deprivation, landlessness, social exclusion.
- High acquittal rates in atrocity cases; underrepresentation.
- Multiple discrimination for SC women (sexual violence). 		- Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989: Punishes caste offenses.
- Post-Matric Scholarship for SC Students: Education support.
- Stand-Up India: Loans for SC entrepreneurs.
- National Commission for Scheduled Castes: Monitoring body.
- Reservation: 15% in jobs/education. 		- 4,09,797 crimes against SCs (NCRB 2014-2022 cumulative); high acquittal (61.7%).
- Poverty decline: Highest in urban SCs (2023-24 data).
- 40-50% reserved seats unfilled due to awareness gaps.
Scheduled Tribes (Adivasis, including PVTGs) - Land rights loss, displacement by projects.
- Exploitation, lack of education/healthcare access.
- Cultural erosion, poverty in remote areas.
- Violence against ST women; ethnic conflicts (e.g., Manipur). 		- Forest Rights Act, 2006: Community forest rights.
- Tribal Sub-Plan (TSP): Development funds.
- Eklavya Model Residential Schools: Education for tribal girls up to Class 12.
- Van Dhan Yojana: Livelihood for minor forest produce.
- Reservation: 7.5% in jobs/education. 		- 8 active AU PSC missions for ST regions (2025).
- PVTGs: 75 groups, facing habitat loss.
- Poverty decline in STs second-highest in urban areas (2023-24).
Other Backward Classes - Social/economic backwardness, limited reservation benefits.
- Unequal sub-caste access (97% benefits to 25% sub-castes).
- Discrimination in higher education/jobs.
- Intersectional issues with gender/minorities. 		- Reservation: 27% in jobs/education (post-Mandal).
- National Backward Classes Finance & Development Corporation: Loans.
- Pre-Matric Scholarship for OBCs.
- Rohini Commission: Equitable OBC sub-categorization. 		- India removed from FATF grey list (2023), aiding OBC finance access.
- 1,000+ OBC communities with no reservation benefits.
- Poverty gap with general category narrowing (2023-24).
Transgender Persons - Social stigma, family rejection, violence.
- Employment discrimination, healthcare barriers (e.g., HRT).
- Legal identity issues, lack of reservations.
- Migration for safety, HIV vulnerability. 		- Transgender Persons (Protection of Rights) Act, 2019: Rights to equality.
- SMILE Scheme: Rehabilitation, skill development, medical facilities.
- National Portal for Transgender Persons: Welfare services.
- Horizontal Reservation: 1% in Karnataka (2021); demands for national vertical quota. 		- 2011 Census: Gender minorities recognized; ongoing 2025 enumeration.
- International Transgender Day (March 31, 2025) highlights challenges.
- 1M+ devices protected via alliances, but schemes underutilized.
Religious Minorities (Muslims, Christians, etc.) - Targeted attacks, forced conversions laws misuse.
- Discrimination in housing/jobs; property demolitions.
- Polarizing policies, vigilante violence.
- Economic exclusion, especially for Muslim women. 		- National Commission for Minorities: Protection oversight.
- Pradhan Mantri Jan Vikas Karyakram: Development in minority areas.
- Waqf (Amendment) Act, 2025: Property management (controversial).
- Scholarships: Pre/Post-Matric for minorities. 		- Muslim-Hindu urban poverty gap reduced from 15% to 2% (2011-24).
- 65 petitions against Waqf Act 2025.
- UN concerns on discrimination (July 2025).
Persons with Disabilities - Accessibility barriers in education, employment, public spaces.
- Healthcare gaps, social stigma, economic dependence.
- Multiple discrimination with caste/gender.
- Underutilization of reservations. 		- Rights of Persons with Disabilities (RPwD) Act, 2016: 21% reservation in govt jobs.
- Accessible India Campaign: Barrier-free environment.
- ADIP Scheme: Aids/appliances distribution.
- National Social Assistance Programme (NSAP): Pensions. 		- 2.68 Cr registered PwDs; low GB allocation for marginalized PwDs.
- Part of UNCRPD implementation; focus on AI ethics (2025).
- Poverty decline inclusive of PwDs (2023-24).
- All acts, policies, welfare schemes, etc in India for women help -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
Constitutional Provisions
Fundamental Rights (Articles 14, 15, 21, 23) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Ensure equality, non-discrimination, and protection of life and liberty for women.
- Scope: Article 14 (equality before law), Article 15 (non-discrimination), Article 21 (right to life/liberty), Article 23 (prohibits trafficking, forced labor).
- Application: Forms the basis for gender equality laws; enforceable via courts. 		- Article 14: Equal treatment under law.
- Article 15: Prohibits discrimination based on sex; enables affirmative action.
- Article 21: Right to dignified life, privacy (e.g., Puttaswamy case, 2017).
- Article 23: Bans human trafficking, bonded labor. 		Landmark judgments: Vishaka (1997), Sabarimala (2018), Triple Talaq (2017); 4.45 lakh cases under women-related laws (NCRB 2024).
Article 42 (Directive Principles) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Secure just and humane work conditions, maternity relief.
- Scope: Non-justiciable but guides welfare policies.
- Application: Influences labor laws, maternity benefits. 		- Provisions: Maternity relief, humane work conditions.
- Implementation: Basis for Maternity Benefit Act, workplace safety laws. 		Supports 26-week maternity leave (2025); 2 Cr women benefited via labor laws (2023-25).
73rd Constitutional Amendment (Article 243D) 1992 Constitution of India - Objective: Empower women in local governance.
- Scope: Mandates reservation for women in Panchayati Raj Institutions.
- Application: Enhances women’s leadership in rural governance. 		- Reservation: 33% seats for women in Panchayats (some states 50%).
- Implementation: Elected women representatives (EWRs) lead local decisions. 		14 lakh women elected in Panchayats (2025); 50% reservation in 21 states; 2025 training programs for EWRs.
Legislative Acts
Women’s Reservation Act (Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam) 2023 Central Legislation - Objective: Ensure women’s representation in Parliament and State Assemblies.
- Scope: Reserves 33% seats for women.
- Application: Applies post-delimitation; enhances political participation. 		- Reservation: 1/3rd seats in Lok Sabha, State Assemblies.
- Implementation: Awaits census, delimitation (expected 2029). 		Passed Sep 2023; 15% women MPs in 17th Lok Sabha (2024); delimitation process initiated (2025).
Prohibition of Child Marriage Act 2006 Central Legislation - Objective: Prevent child marriages, protect girls’ rights.
- Scope: Sets minimum marriage age (21 for men, 18 for women).
- Application: Applies to all communities; voids underage marriages. 		- Provisions: Punishment up to 2 years imprisonment; Child Marriage Prohibition Officers (Sections 3-16).
- Protection: Maintenance for minor girls; voidable marriages. 		1.4 lakh child marriages prevented (2023-24); 23% girls married below 18 (NFHS-5); 2025 awareness drives intensified.
Protection of Women from Domestic Violence Act (PWDVA) 2005 Central Legislation - Objective: Protect women from domestic violence (physical, emotional, economic).
- Scope: Covers women in domestic relationships.
- Application: Provides civil remedies, support services. 		- Remedies: Protection, residence, monetary, custody orders (Sections 18-23).
- Mechanisms: Protection Officers, service providers (Sections 8-12).
- Penalties: Breach punishable by 1-year imprisonment (Section 31). 		802 One-Stop Centres (2025); 4.45 lakh cases annually (NCRB 2024); Supreme Court pushes implementation (May 2025).
Sexual Harassment of Women at Workplace (POSH) Act 2013 Central Legislation - Objective: Prevent and redress workplace sexual harassment.
- Scope: All workplaces, including unorganized sector.
- Application: Mandates Internal Complaints Committees (ICCs). 		- Mechanisms: ICC for 10+ employee organizations; inquiry within 90 days (Sections 4, 11).
- Remedies: Conciliation, compensation, transfer; ₹50,000 fine for non-compliance (Sections 13-26). 		60% workplaces non-compliant with ICC (2024); 30,000+ complaints resolved (2023-25); 2025 compliance audits planned.
Maternity Benefit (Amendment) Act 2017 Central Legislation - Objective: Enhance maternity benefits for working women.
- Scope: Covers establishments with 10+ employees.
- Application: Ensures paid leave, workplace facilities. 		- Benefits: 26 weeks paid maternity leave; 12 weeks for adoption/surrogacy (Section 5).
- Facilities: Creche for 50+ employees; work-from-home options (Section 11A). 		2 Cr women benefited (2023-25); 70% compliance in formal sector; 2025 focus on unorganized sector.
Welfare Schemes
Mission Shakti 2021 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Umbrella scheme for women’s safety, empowerment, and protection.
- Scope: Integrates schemes like OSC, WHL, Swadhar Greh, BBBP.
- Application: Holistic approach to address violence, economic empowerment. 		- Components: Sambal (safety/protection), Samarthya (empowerment).
- Sub-Schemes: OSCs, WHL, Nari Adalat, BBBP, PMMVY.
- Implementation: State/district-level coordination; Nirbhaya Fund support. 		₹5,000 Cr allocated (2024-25); 12 lakh women assisted via Sambal; 2 Cr reached via Samarthya (2025).
Beti Bachao Beti Padhao (BBBP) 2015 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Address declining child sex ratio, promote girl child education.
- Scope: Nationwide campaign; focuses on awareness, education.
- Application: Targets districts with low CSR. 		- Components: Awareness campaigns, education incentives.
- Implementation: Multi-ministerial (WCD, Education, Health). 		CSR improved from 918 to 933 (2015-24); 1 Cr girls enrolled in schools; 2025 digital campaigns launched.
Sukanya Samriddhi Yojana 2015 Ministry of Finance - Objective: Promote savings for girl child’s education, marriage.
- Scope: Savings scheme for girls under 10 years.
- Application: High-interest accounts in post offices/banks. 		- Benefits: 8.2% interest rate; ₹250-₹1.5 lakh annual deposit.
- Taxation: EEE (Exempt-Exempt-Exempt) status; maturity at 21 years. 		3.2 Cr accounts opened; ₹2.1 lakh Cr mobilized (2025); 2025 rural outreach expanded.
Udaan Scheme 2014 Ministry of Skill Development - Objective: Provide skill training, employment to women.
- Scope: Targets women graduates, professionals.
- Application: Focuses on corporate sector placement. 		- Training: 100-hour programs; soft skills, IT.
- Placement: Corporate tie-ups; mentorship support. 		50,000+ women trained; 60% placed in jobs (2023-25); 2025 focus on tech skills.
One Stop Centre Scheme 2015 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Provide integrated support to women facing violence.
- Scope: Single-window access to legal, medical, counseling services.
- Application: Nationwide network of centres. 		- Services: Medical aid, police assistance, legal aid, shelter.
- Operation: 24/7 helpline (181); Sakhi centres. 		802 centres operational; 10 lakh women assisted; 2025 integration with ERSS-112 enhanced.
Swadhar Greh & Short Stay Homes 2015 (Swadhar); 1966 (Short Stay) Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Provide shelter, rehabilitation for women in distress.
- Scope: Targets destitute, trafficked, homeless women.
- Application: Offers temporary housing, skill training. 		- Services: Shelter, food, medical care, vocational training.
- Capacity: 30-50 women per home; 6-month stay (extendable). 		410 Swadhar Grehs; 1.2 lakh women rehabilitated (2023-25); 2025 focus on trafficking victims.
Nirbhaya Fund 2013 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Fund initiatives for women’s safety, empowerment.
- Scope: Supports projects like OSC, safe cities, helplines.
- Application: Multi-ministerial allocation. 		- Funding: ₹7,713 Cr corpus; supports 360 projects.
- Projects: Safe City, ERSS (112), women’s hostels, fast-track courts. 		₹5,118 Cr utilized (2024); 2 Cr calls via 112 helpline; 1,000 new fast-track courts proposed (2025).
Pradhan Mantri Mudra Yojana (PMMY) 2015 Ministry of Finance - Objective: Provide microfinance to women entrepreneurs.
- Scope: Non-corporate, non-farm small enterprises.
- Application: Loans for small businesses, startups. 		- Loans: Shishu (up to ₹50,000), Kishore (₹50,000-₹5 lakh), Tarun (₹5-10 lakh).
- Access: Collateral-free; priority to women. 		68% beneficiaries women; ₹23 lakh Cr disbursed; 2025 digital loan platform launched.
Deendayal Antyodaya Yojana 2015 Ministry of Rural Development - Objective: Empower women via Self-Help Groups (SHGs).
- Scope: Rural and urban poor; livelihood promotion.
- Application: Microfinance, skill training for women. 		- SHGs: 10-20 women groups; credit linkage.
- Training: Skill development, market access. 		9 Cr women in 81 lakh SHGs; ₹2 lakh Cr credit linked; 2025 e-commerce platforms for SHGs.
Stand Up India Scheme 2016 Ministry of Finance - Objective: Promote entrepreneurship among women, SC/ST.
- Scope: Loans for greenfield enterprises.
- Application: Supports women-led startups. 		- Loans: ₹10 lakh-₹1 Cr; composite loans.
- Support: Handholding, credit guarantee. 		80% beneficiaries women; 1.8 lakh enterprises funded; 2025 focus on women in tech startups.
Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (PMJAY) 2018 Ministry of Health - Objective: Provide healthcare access to women, poor families.
- Scope: Covers 50 Cr people; secondary/tertiary care.
- Application: Cashless treatment for women. 		- Coverage: ₹5 lakh/family/year for hospitalization.
- Access: 27,000+ empanelled hospitals. 		55% women beneficiaries; 3 Cr treatments; 2025 women-specific health camps launched.
Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana 2016 Ministry of Petroleum - Objective: Provide clean cooking fuel to women in BPL households.
- Scope: LPG connections for rural/poor women.
- Application: Reduces health risks from biomass fuel. 		- Benefits: Free LPG connection; subsidy on refills.
- Implementation: Targets 8 Cr households. 		10 Cr connections issued; 80% women-led households covered; 2025 refill subsidy increased.
Women Helpline Scheme (WHL) 2015 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Provide 24/7 emergency support for women facing violence.
- Scope: Nationwide helpline (181) linked to OSCs, ERSS-112.
- Application: Immediate assistance, referrals for legal/medical aid. 		- Services: Emergency response, counseling, referrals.
- Operation: State/district-level call centres; integrated with Mission Shakti. 		2 Cr calls handled (2023-25); 80% resolution rate; 2025 AI-based call analytics introduced.
Mahila Samman Savings Certificate 2023 Ministry of Finance - Objective: Encourage financial inclusion for women via savings.
- Scope: Women-specific small savings scheme.
- Application: High-interest savings for women/girls of any age. 		- Benefits: 7.5% interest rate; ₹1,000-₹2 lakh deposit; 2-year tenure.
- Access: Available at post offices, banks; partial withdrawal after 1 year. 		1.5 Cr accounts opened; ₹50,000 Cr mobilized (2025); 2025 rural awareness campaigns launched.
Nari Adalat 2023 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Provide alternative dispute resolution for women’s issues.
- Scope: Community-based grievance redressal under Mission Shakti.
- Application: Focuses on domestic violence, property disputes, social issues. 		- Mechanism: Women-led forums for mediation, counseling.
- Implementation: Operates at Gram Panchayat level; linked to NCW. 		1,000+ Nari Adalats operational; 50,000+ cases resolved (2024-25); 2025 expansion to 5,000 units.
Pradhan Mantri Matru Vandana Yojana (PMMVY) 2017 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Provide financial support for pregnant/lactating mothers.
- Scope: Cash incentives for first living child.
- Application: Supports maternal health, nutrition. 		- Benefits: ₹5,000 in installments for first child; ₹6,000 for second girl child (select states).
- Implementation: DBT via Aadhaar-linked accounts. 		3 Cr mothers benefited; ₹12,000 Cr disbursed (2023-25); 2025 digital enrollment simplified.
- All bodies, agencies, etc in India for women help -
Body/Agency Year Established Authority Purpose/Description Key Functions/Activities Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
National Level Bodies
National Commission for Women (NCW) 1992 Statutory Body (NCW Act, 1990) - Objective: Protect and promote women's rights, advise on policy matters.
- Scope: Reviews constitutional/legal safeguards, facilitates grievance redressal.
- Application: Addresses dowry, violence, political participation, employment issues. 		- Core Functions: Investigate complaints (suo moto/oral/written), recommend legislative changes, monitor law implementation.
- Activities: Gender sensitization workshops, legal awareness, Parivarik Mahila Lok Adalats, advisories on institutional safety.
- Structure: Chairperson, 5 members; civil court powers for inquiries. 		Chairperson: Vijaya Rahatkar (2024); 7,500+ cases handled; J&K/Ladakh Cell established; 2025 focus on child marriage, cybercrimes; 2-day counselor training (Oct 2025).
Rashtriya Mahila Kosh (RMK) 1993 Autonomous Body (MWCD) - Objective: Empower poor/rural women via micro-credit.
- Scope: Collateral-free loans for income-generating activities.
- Application: Targets unorganized sector women through SHGs/NGOs. 		- Core Activities: Microfinance, enterprise development, thrift/credit linkages.
- Programs: Loans via intermediary microfinance organizations (IMOs); capacity building.
- Structure: Society under Societies Registration Act, 1860. 		Discontinued 2021 due to PMMY overlap; legacy impact: ₹2.1 lakh Cr mobilized, 3.2 Cr accounts; 1M+ women benefited via SHGs (pre-2025).
Central Social Welfare Board (CSWB) 1953 Autonomous Body (MWCD) - Objective: Promote voluntarism, welfare for women, children, families.
- Scope: Financial/technical aid to voluntary organizations.
- Application: Addresses moral danger, family discord, exploitation. 		- Core Activities: Grants for NGOs (counseling, creches, training).
- Programs: Short Stay Homes, Awareness Generation, Social Evils campaigns; industrial cooperatives.
- Structure: Chairperson-led; 56-member General Body, 16-member Executive Committee. 		1.2 lakh women rehabilitated (2023-25); ₹100+ Cr annual funds; 2024 focus on gender awareness, child welfare.
Savitribai Phule National Institute of Women and Child Development (SPNIWCD, formerly NIPCCD) 1966 Autonomous Institute (MWCD) - Objective: Promote voluntary action, research, training in women/child development.
- Scope: Capacity building for ICDS, policy research.
- Application: Apex training body for ICDS, women’s welfare programs. 		- Core Functions: Training, research, documentation; national/international partnerships.
- Activities: Gender workshops, child rights training; regional outreach.
- Structure: Delhi HQ; 6 regional centres (Guwahati, Bengaluru, Lucknow, Indore, Mohali, Ranchi). 		Renamed Jul 2025; Ranchi centre opened (2025); trained 1M+ functionaries; 2025 internship scheme; focus on AI ethics in welfare.
Nirbhaya Fund Management Committee (Empowered Committee) 2013 Inter-Ministerial Body (MWCD/MoF) - Objective: Administer fund for women’s safety post-2012 Delhi case.
- Scope: Supports violence prevention, security projects.
- Application: Funds OSCs, safe cities, helplines. 		- Core Activities: Project appraisal, monitoring.
- Programs: 49 schemes (ERSS-112, Fast Track Courts, OSCs).
- Structure: Chaired by MWCD Secretary; MoF/DEA nodal agency. 		₹7,713 Cr allocated (2024-25); 76% utilized (₹5,118 Cr); 10 lakh women assisted via OSCs; extended to 2025-26.
Women Helpline Scheme (WHL) 2015 MWCD (under Mission Shakti) - Objective: Provide 24/7 emergency support for women facing violence.
- Scope: Nationwide helpline (181) linked to OSCs, ERSS-112.
- Application: Immediate assistance, referrals for legal/medical aid. 		- Core Functions: Emergency response, coordination with police, OSCs.
- Activities: Counseling, referrals, awareness drives.
- Structure: Operates via state/district-level call centres. 		2 Cr calls handled (2023-25); integrated with ERSS-112; 80% resolution rate (2024).
National Mission for Empowerment of Women (NMEW) 2010 MWCD - Objective: Strengthen inter-sectoral convergence for women’s empowerment.
- Scope: Coordinates schemes across ministries for holistic development.
- Application: Focuses on economic, social, and legal empowerment. 		- Core Functions: Policy advocacy, convergence of schemes (e.g., BBBP, PMMY).
- Activities: Gender budgeting, Poorna Shakti Kendras for grassroots outreach.
- Structure: MWCD-led; state/district-level convergence groups. 		Subsumed under Mission Shakti (2021); 1 Cr+ women reached via convergence (pre-2025); 2025 focus on digital literacy.
State and Local Level Bodies
State Women Commissions Varies (e.g., 1993 Maharashtra) Statutory Bodies (State Acts) - Objective: Protect/promote women’s rights at state level.
- Scope: 29 states/UTs (e.g., Andhra Pradesh, Delhi, UP).
- Application: Addresses local issues like violence, trafficking. 		- Core Functions: Investigate complaints, recommend policies, monitor safeguards.
- Activities: Legal aid, awareness camps, NCW coordination.
- Structure: Chairperson + members (law/social work experts); civil court powers. 		29 states/UTs active (2025); Delhi: 30,000+ complaints resolved (2023-25); Northeast focus on trafficking.
District and Block Level Women Welfare Committees Varies (state schemes) Local Government Bodies - Objective: Grassroots implementation of women’s welfare programs.
- Scope: Coordinates schemes like ICDS, POSH, OSCs.
- Application: Local grievance redressal, awareness drives. 		- Core Activities: Scheme monitoring, community mobilization, counseling.
- Programs: SHG linkages, gender budgeting, OSC coordination.
- Structure: Under District Magistrates/Block Development Officers. 		700+ districts (2025); 5 lakh+ women assisted (2024); integrated with Mission Shakti.
State Resource Centre for Women (SRCW) 2011 State Governments (under NMEW/Mission Shakti) - Objective: Facilitate women’s empowerment through state-level coordination.
- Scope: Implements national schemes, monitors gender programs.
- Application: Focuses on rural women, SHGs, and skill development. 		- Core Functions: Policy implementation, data collection, training.
- Activities: Support for BBBP, PMMY; gender sensitization workshops.
- Structure: State nodal agencies under MWCD guidelines. 		33 SRCWs operational (2025); 2 lakh+ women trained in skills (2023-25); supports Mission Shakti outreach.
Specialized Committees/Task Forces
Gender Budgeting Cells (GBCs) 2005 Various Ministries (MWCD-led) - Objective: Mainstream gender in budgetary allocations.
- Scope: Ensures women-centric funding across ministries.
- Application: Enhances scheme effectiveness for women. 		- Core Functions: Allocate funds for women-specific/partially benefiting schemes.
- Activities: Gender Budget Statement in Union Budget; capacity building.
- Structure: Cells in 56 ministries/departments. 		₹2.6 lakh Cr in Gender Budget (2024-25); 30% schemes women-specific; 2025 focus on health, education.
National Task Force on Violence Against Women 2013 (revived periodically) MWCD - Objective: Address rising violence against women, recommend reforms.
- Scope: Reviews laws like PWDVA, POCSO; suggests policy changes.
- Application: Focuses on systemic gaps in justice delivery. 		- Core Functions: Policy recommendations, stakeholder consultations.
- Activities: Reports on fast-track courts, helplines; advises on Nirbhaya Fund.
- Structure: Inter-ministerial; includes NGOs, experts. 		Revived 2024; recommended 1,000 new fast-track courts; influenced 2025 budget allocations.
- All policies, acts, welfare schemes, etc in India for children help -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
Constitutional Provisions
Article 39(f) (Directive Principles) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Ensure children are given opportunities and facilities to develop in a healthy manner and in conditions of freedom and dignity.
- Scope: Protects children from exploitation and moral/national abandonment.
- Application: Guides child welfare policies; non-justiciable but foundational for laws like JJ Act. 		- Provisions: State to direct policy towards child development, protection from abuse.
- Implementation: Influences ICDS, POSHAN, and child labor bans. 		Supports 14 lakh children via child protection schemes (2025); integrated into Mission Vatsalya (2023-25).
Article 21A (Fundamental Rights) 2002 Constitution of India - Objective: Provide free and compulsory education for children aged 6-14 years.
- Scope: Right to education as fundamental right.
- Application: Enforceable via RTE Act; promotes universal access. 		- Provisions: Free education, no detention up to Class 8 (amended).
- Implementation: 25% reservation in private schools for disadvantaged children. 		96% enrollment in elementary education (2024); 2.5 Cr children benefited via Samagra Shiksha (2023-25).
Articles 23 & 24 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Prohibit trafficking, forced labor, and employment of children in hazardous jobs.
- Scope: Article 23 bans human trafficking/bonded labor; Article 24 prohibits child labor under 14 in factories/mines.
- Application: Basis for Child Labour Act; protects vulnerable children. 		- Provisions: Punishment for violations; rehabilitation for rescued children.
- Implementation: Links to JJ Act, NCLP for education/rehab. 		1.1 lakh children rescued from labor (2023-24); 50,000 rehabilitated via NCLP (2025).
Article 45 (Directive Principles) 1950 (amended) Constitution of India - Objective: Provide early childhood care and education for children below 6 years.
- Scope: Free/compulsory education up to 14; ECCE for under-6s.
- Application: Guides ICDS, NEP 2020 for foundational learning. 		- Provisions: State to minimize neglect, provide opportunities for healthy development.
- Implementation: ECCE in Anganwadis; integrated with POSHAN 2.0. 		1.4 Cr children covered under ECCE (2025); 80% Anganwadis upgraded (2024).
Legislative Acts
Juvenile Justice (Care and Protection of Children) Act (JJ Act) 2015 Central Legislation - Objective: Provide care, protection, and justice for children in need/conflict with law.
- Scope: Covers adoption, foster care, juvenile justice boards.
- Application: Replaces 2000 Act; focuses on rehabilitation. 		- Provisions: Child Welfare Committees (CWCs), Juvenile Justice Boards (JJBs); offenses against children (Sections 74-79).
- Implementation: Sponsorship, aftercare; aligns with UNCRC. 		2.5 lakh children in CCIs benefited (2024); 2025 amendments for digital tracking; 90% disposal rate in JJBs.
Prohibition of Child Marriage Act 2006 Central Legislation - Objective: Prevent child marriages, protect children's rights to health/education.
- Scope: Minimum age 21 (boys), 18 (girls); voids underage marriages.
- Application: Applies nationwide; empowers Child Marriage Prohibition Officers. 		- Provisions: Penalties up to 2 years imprisonment/fine (Sections 3-16); maintenance for victims.
- Implementation: Awareness via BBBP, ICDS. 		1.4 lakh marriages prevented (2023-24); 23% girls married below 18 (NFHS-5); 2025 campaigns in high-risk districts.
Protection of Children from Sexual Offences (POCSO) Act 2012 Central Legislation - Objective: Protect children from sexual abuse, exploitation, pornography.
- Scope: Covers 0-18 years; child-friendly procedures.
- Application: Special courts, fast-track trials. 		- Provisions: Criminalizes offenses (penetrative assault, harassment); mandatory reporting (Sections 19-21).
- Implementation: POCSO e-Box for online complaints; child-friendly courts. 		62,000 cases registered (2023); 1,000 fast-track courts (2025); 70% conviction rate improved (2024).
Right to Education (RTE) Act 2009 Central Legislation - Objective: Implement Article 21A for free/compulsory education (6-14 years).
- Scope: Neighborhood schools, no capitation fees.
- Application: Regulates school standards, teacher norms. 		- Provisions: 25% reservation for disadvantaged; no detention till Class 8 (amended 2019).
- Implementation: Monitored via U-DISE; aligns with NEP 2020. 		1.2 Cr children admitted under 25% quota (2023-25); 95% gross enrollment ratio (2024).
Welfare Schemes
Integrated Child Development Services (ICDS) 1975 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Holistic development of 0-6 year children via Anganwadis.
- Scope: Nutrition, health, pre-school education for children; support for mothers.
- Application: World's largest early childhood program; 14 lakh Anganwadis. 		- Components: Supplementary nutrition, immunization, health check-ups, ECCE.
- Implementation: Anganwadi Workers (AWWs); integrated with POSHAN 2.0. 		10 Cr beneficiaries (2025); 1.37 lakh Anganwadis upgraded (2024); stunting reduced by 7% (2023-25).
POSHAN Abhiyaan (POSHAN 2.0) 2018 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Reduce malnutrition, stunting, anemia in children/women.
- Scope: Targets 0-6 years children, adolescent girls, pregnant/lactating mothers.
- Application: Technology-driven; Jan Andolan for behavior change. 		- Components: Growth monitoring via Poshan Tracker; Poshan Vatikas; incentives for AWWs.
- Implementation: Convergence with ICDS, NHM; annual Poshan Maah/Pakhwada. 		₹21,960 Cr allocated (2025-26); 5 Cr children monitored; stunting down 5% (2023-25); 7th Poshan Maah (Sep 2024).
Samagra Shiksha Abhiyan 2018 Ministry of Education - Objective: Holistic school education from pre-school to Class 12.
- Scope: Integrates SSA, RMSA; equity for girls, disabled children.
- Application: Improves learning outcomes, infrastructure. 		- Components: ECCE, vocational education, digital learning (DIKSHA).
- Implementation: Composite school grants; teacher training. 		₹37,500 Cr budget (2024-25); 1 Cr schools strengthened; 90% retention rate (2025).
National Education Policy (NEP) 2020 2020 Ministry of Education - Objective: Transform India's education system for holistic, flexible learning.
- Scope: 5+3+3+4 structure; focus on foundational literacy/numeracy.
- 		Application: Multilingualism, vocational integration; aligns with RTE. - Provisions: NIPUN Bharat for FLN by Class 3; 6% GDP on education.
- Implementation: 10,000 Atal Tinkering Labs; PM SHRI schools. 		2 Cr teachers trained (2023-25); 50% schools NEP-aligned (2025); enrollment up 5%.
Mission Vatsalya 2021 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Strengthen child protection, welfare services.
- Scope: Replaces ICPS; covers foster care, adoption, aftercare.
- Application: Family-based care over institutional; aligns with JJ Act. 		- Components: TrackChild portal; sponsorship for 6-18 years.
- Implementation: State Child Protection Societies; ₹1,391 Cr (2025-26). 		1.5 lakh children protected (2024); 5,000 foster families (2025); 95% family reintegration.
PM Poshan Shakti Nirman (PM POSHAN) / Mid-Day Meal Scheme 2021 Ministry of Education - Objective: Enhance nutrition, enrollment, attendance via school meals.
- Scope: Hot cooked meals for Classes 1-8 in govt. schools.
- Application: Includes millets, fortified foods; covers bal vatikas. 		- Provisions: 480g food grains/child/day; nutrition gardens.
- Implementation: FSSAI standards; DBT for cooking costs. 		11.8 Cr children benefited (2024); 11.20 Cr meals/day; anemia reduced 10% (2023-25).
Shala Praveshotsav 2010 (ongoing) Ministry of Education / State-led - Objective: Boost enrollment in schools, especially marginalized children.
- Scope: Annual door-to-door campaign for school admissions.
- 		Application: Focus on dropouts, out-of-school children; integrates with RTE. - Components: Community mobilization, enrollment drives.
- Implementation: Village-level events; tracking via U-DISE. 		2 lakh children enrolled annually (2023-24); 95% retention in Assam/Gujarat (2025).
Thalliki Vandanam Scheme 2025 Government of Andhra Pradesh - Objective: Financial aid to mothers for children's education (Class 1-12).
- Scope: Reduces dropout rates in low-income families.
- Application: Direct benefit transfer to mothers' accounts. 		- Benefits: ₹15,000/year per child; covers tuition, supplies.
- Implementation: Aadhaar-linked DBT; eligibility for BPL/white ration families. 		67 lakh mothers benefited; ₹8,745 Cr allocated (2025-26); dropout rate down 15% (2025).
Balika Samriddhi Yojana 1997 Ministry of Women & Child Development - Objective: Promote girl child survival, education in BPL families.
- Scope: Post-delivery financial aid, scholarships till Class 10.
- Application: Improves CSR, reduces discrimination. 		- Benefits: ₹500 post-delivery + annual scholarships (₹300-1,000); uniform allowance.
- Implementation: Post offices/banks; up to two girls per family. 		10 lakh girls benefited annually (2024); integrated with BBBP; CSR improved 5% (2023-25).
PM CARES for Children Scheme 2021 PM CARES Fund - Objective: Support children orphaned/lost parents to COVID-19.
- Scope: Holistic care till 23 years; health, education, foster care.
- Application: Supplements JJ Act; guardian appointment. 		- Benefits: Health insurance (₹5 lakh), education fund; monthly stipend post-18.
- Implementation: District Collectors oversee; digital portal. 		4,000+ children supported (2025); ₹15,000 Cr corpus; 100% coverage for eligible (2024).
National Child Labour Project (NCLP) 1988 Ministry of Labour & Employment - Objective: Rehabilitate child laborers via education, vocational training.
- Scope: Special schools for 5-14 year working children.
- Application: Focus on high-incidence districts. 		- Components: Bridge courses, mainstreaming; family counseling.
- Implementation: 300+ projects; aligns with Child Labour Act. 		1 lakh children mainstreamed (2023-25); 50,000 in NCLP schools (2025).
- All bodies, agencies, etc in India for children help -
Body/Agency Year Established Authority Purpose/Description Key Functions/Activities Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
National Level Bodies
National Commission for Protection of Child Rights (NCPCR) 2007 Statutory Body (CPCR Act, 2005) - Objective: Protect and promote child rights; monitor implementation of child-related laws.
- Scope: Apex body for education, health, protection from abuse; advises on JJ Act, POCSO, RTE.
- Application: Investigates violations, recommends policy reforms. 		- Core Functions: Complaint redressal (suo moto/oral/written), policy advocacy, annual reports.
- Activities: Fact-finding missions, awareness campaigns, GHAR portal for missing children, E-Baal Nidan portal.
- Structure: Chairperson + 6 members; civil court powers for inquiries. 		1 lakh complaints received (Apr-Dec 2024); 39,500 on juvenile justice; 18,954 resolved; E-Baal Nidan revamped (2022, active 2025); 2025 focus on cybercrime against children.
Central Adoption Resource Authority (CARA) 2010 Statutory Body (MWCD) - Objective: Regulate and facilitate adoptions of orphan/abandoned/surrendered children.
- Scope: Nodal agency for in-country/inter-country adoptions under Hague Convention.
- Application: Oversees Specialized Adoption Agencies (SAAs). 		- Core Functions: Register Prospective Adoptive Parents (PAPs), child matching, post-adoption monitoring.
- Activities: CARINGS portal, training for SAAs, counseling; guidelines for relative adoptions.
- Structure: MWCD-attached; helpline 1800-11-1311. 		4,515 adoptions (FY 2024-25), highest in 12 years; 4,155 domestic, 360 inter-country; 60% girls, 80% aged 0-2; 30,000+ PAPs registered (2025).
Savitribai Phule National Institute of Women and Child Development (SPNIWCD, formerly NIPCCD) 1966 Autonomous Institute (MWCD) - Objective: Capacity building, research, and training for child development/protection.
- Scope: Apex training body for ICDS, POSHAN, Mission Vatsalya.
- Application: Supports functionaries in child welfare programs. 		- Core Functions: Training, policy research, documentation; national/international partnerships.
- Activities: Advanced Diploma in Child Guidance, JJ Act/POCSO workshops; Poshan Bhi Padhai Bhi PMU.
- Structure: Delhi HQ; 6 regional centres (Guwahati, Bengaluru, Lucknow, Indore, Mohali, Ranchi). 		Renamed Jul 2025; Ranchi centre opened (Jul 2025); 1M+ functionaries trained; 2025 internship scheme; 10,000+ AWWs trained in ECCE (2024).
Ministry of Women and Child Development (MWCD) 2006 Central Ministry - Objective: Formulate and implement policies for child welfare, nutrition, protection.
- Scope: Oversees ICDS, POSHAN, Mission Vatsalya, adoptions, child rights.
- Application: Coordinates with states, funds schemes, monitors outcomes. 		- Core Functions: Policy-making, funding allocation, state-level coordination.
- Activities: Poshan Pakhwada, Palna crèches, Vatsalya portal, Childline oversight.
- Structure: Secretary-led; divisions for nutrition, protection, education. 		₹21,960 Cr for POSHAN 2.0 (2025-26); 11,395 AWCCs approved, 1,761 operational (Mar 2025); 7th Poshan Pakhwada (Apr 2025).
Childline India Foundation (CIF) / Childline 1098 1996 NGO under MWCD - Objective: Provide 24/7 emergency helpline for children in distress.
- Scope: Covers abuse, trafficking, missing children; links to police, CWCs.
- Application: Nationwide outreach; integrated with ERSS-112. 		- Core Functions: Rescue, rehabilitation, counseling; awareness campaigns.
- Activities: 60-minute response time; multilingual support; data tracking.
- Structure: Operates via 700+ partner NGOs; call centres in 600+ cities. 		2 Cr calls handled (2023-25); 1.5 lakh children rescued; 80% cases linked to CWCs (2024); AI-based analytics introduced (2025).
National Child Labour Project (NCLP) 1988 Ministry of Labour & Employment - Objective: Rehabilitate child laborers through education and vocational training.
- Scope: Targets children in hazardous work; operates special schools.
- Application: Focuses on high-incidence districts; aligns with Child Labour Act. 		- Core Functions: Bridge education, mainstreaming to regular schools; family counseling.
- Activities: Surveys, rehabilitation programs, stipends for children.
- Structure: Project societies in 300+ districts; state-level monitoring. 		1 lakh children mainstreamed (2023-25); 50,000 in NCLP schools; 2025 digital tracking system launched.
National Authority for Chemical Weapons Convention (NACWC) 1998 Cabinet Secretariat - Objective: Protect children from exposure to hazardous chemicals in workplaces.
- Scope: Enforces ban on child labor in chemical-related industries.
- Application: Supports Article 24; coordinates with NCLP, MWCD. 		- Core Functions: Inspect chemical industries, enforce CWC compliance.
- Activities: Training on safety, child labor audits, rehabilitation support.
- Structure: Inter-ministerial body; nodal for CWC implementation. 		10,000+ inspections (2023-24); 5,000 children rescued from hazardous work; 2025 safety guidelines updated.
State Level Bodies
State Commissions for Protection of Child Rights (SCPCRs) Varies (e.g., 2007 onwards) Statutory Bodies (State CPCR Acts) - Objective: Protect child rights at state level; mirror NCPCR functions.
- Scope: 29 states/UTs; monitor JJ Act, POCSO, RTE implementation.
- Application: Address local issues like trafficking, education access. 		- Core Functions: Investigate complaints, recommend state policies, monitor safeguards.
- Activities: Awareness drives, coordination with NCPCR, Childline.
- Structure: Chairperson + members; civil court powers. 		29 SCPCRs active (2025); 50,000+ complaints resolved (2023-25); e.g., Maharashtra SCPCR handled 10,000 cases (2024).
State Child Protection Societies (SCPS) 2009 MWCD (under Mission Vatsalya) - Objective: Implement child protection services at state level.
- Scope: Coordinates Mission Vatsalya, ICPS; supports CWCs, JJBs.
- Application: Focuses on family-based care, rehabilitation. 		- Core Functions: Fund allocation, training, monitoring DCPUs.
- Activities: Sponsorship, foster care programs; TrackChild portal.
- Structure: State nodal agencies; report to MWCD. 		36 SCPS operational (2025); 1.5 lakh children supported via foster care/sponsorship; 95% family reintegration (2024).
District and Local Level Bodies
Child Welfare Committees (CWCs) 2000 (under JJ Act) State Governments (JJ Act, 2015) - Objective: Provide care/protection for children in need (orphans, abused, abandoned).
- Scope: District-level quasi-judicial bodies under JJ Act.
- Application: Declares children legally free for adoption; rehabilitation. 		- Core Functions: Case management, rehabilitation plans, adoption orders.
- Activities: Counseling, family tracing; link with DCPUs, SAAs.
- Structure: Chairperson + 4 members; 700+ districts. 		2.5 lakh children in CCIs supported (2024); 80% cases disposed within 4 months; 2025 digital case tracking introduced.
Juvenile Justice Boards (JJBs) 2000 (under JJ Act) State Governments (JJ Act, 2015) - Objective: Adjudicate cases of children in conflict with law.
- Scope: Handles juvenile offenses; ensures child-friendly procedures.
- Application: Focuses on reformation over punishment. 		- Core Functions: Bail hearings, trials, rehabilitation plans.
- Activities: Counseling, vocational training; links with observation homes.
- Structure: Magistrate + 2 social workers; 700+ districts. 		90% case disposal rate (2024); 50,000 juveniles rehabilitated; 2025 focus on restorative justice programs.
District Child Protection Units (DCPUs) 2009 MWCD (under Mission Vatsalya) - Objective: Implement child protection services at district level.
- Scope: Coordinates CWCs, JJBs, Childline; supports Mission Vatsalya.
- Application: Grassroots case management, rescue operations. 		- Core Functions: Monitor CCIs, coordinate rescues, awareness programs.
- Activities: Foster care, sponsorship; data collection via TrackChild.
- Structure: District Magistrate-led; multi-disciplinary team. 		700+ DCPUs operational (2025); 1 lakh children rescued/rehabilitated; 2025 digital MIS for case tracking.
District Child Protection Committees (DCPCs) 2009 MWCD (under Mission Vatsalya) - Objective: Coordinate child welfare/protection at district level.
- Scope: Oversees DCPUs, ensures scheme convergence.
- Application: Links ICDS, POSHAN, JJ Act implementation. 		- Core Functions: Policy oversight, resource allocation, monitoring.
- Activities: Community awareness, school safety programs, child labor surveys.
- Structure: Chaired by District Collector; includes NGOs, officials. 		700+ DCPCs active (2025); 2 lakh children reached via awareness drives; 2025 child safety audits strengthened.
Program-Specific Implementation Bodies
Integrated Child Protection Scheme (ICPS) Implementing Bodies 2009 MWCD (subsumed under Mission Vatsalya) - Objective: Create a protective environment for children via integrated services.
- Scope: Supports CWCs, JJBs, DCPUs, Childline; now part of Mission Vatsalya.
- Application: Focuses on prevention, rehabilitation, reintegration. 		- Core Functions: Fund and monitor protection services; capacity building.
- Activities: Sponsorship, foster care, CCI inspections; TrackChild portal.
- Structure: SCPS, DCPUs, CWCs, JJBs as implementing arms. 		Subsumed under Mission Vatsalya (2021); 1.5 lakh children supported (2024); 95% family-based care achieved (2025).

National Policies
National Youth Policy (NYP) 2014 (Draft NYP 2024) 2014 (Draft 2024) Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports - Objective: Empower youth (15-29 years) to realize full potential; align with SDGs for holistic development.
- Scope: Covers education, employment, health, leadership, social justice; updated draft focuses on 10-year vision to 2034.
- Application: Guides state policies; promotes volunteering, tech empowerment, mental health. 		- Objectives: 5 key areas (employment, leadership, health, social justice, sustainability); 11 priority sectors.
- Implementation: Integrates with NEP 2020, Skill India; public consultations for draft (2024). 		Draft released 2024; 1 lakh+ youth consultations; aligns with Viksit Bharat@2047; 66% population under 35 empowered via linked schemes (2025).
National Education Policy (NEP) 2020 (Youth Focus) 2020 Ministry of Education - Objective: Transform education for skill-based, holistic youth development; increase GER to 50% by 2035.
- Scope: 5+3+3+4 structure; vocational training for 50% students by 2025; digital learning.
- Application: Empowers youth via FLN, multilingualism, entrepreneurship education. 		- Provisions: NIPUN Bharat for foundational literacy; 6% GDP on education; PM SHRI schools.
- Implementation: Atal Tinkering Labs, DIKSHA platform; aligns with NSDM. 		2 Cr teachers trained; 50% schools aligned (2025); 100M learners on digital platforms; GER up 5% (2023-25).
Skill Development Schemes
National Skill Development Mission (NSDM) 2015 Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship - Objective: Enable India as skill-based economy; train 40 Cr youth by 2022 (extended).
- Scope: Domain/accreditation standards, vocational training, industry partnerships.
- Application: Integrates PMKVY, DDU-GKY; focuses on employability. 		- Components: Skill Qualification Framework, e-Skill India; convergence with states.
- Implementation: NSDC oversight; short-term training programs. 		1.4 Cr youth trained (2023-25); 70% placement rate; ₹22.5 lakh Cr for youth entrepreneurship (2025).
Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana (PMKVY) 2015 Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship - Objective: Skill certification for industry-relevant jobs; short-term training for youth.
- : Targets school/college dropouts, unemployed youth; recognition of prior learning.
- Application: Free training in 300+ job roles; monetary rewards. 		- Provisions: Recognition of Prior Learning (RPL), on-job training; 40-hour courses.
- Implementation: Via training centers; digital tracking. 		1.2 Cr youth certified; 60% employed (2023-25); PMKVY 4.0 launched 2024 with AI focus.
Deendayal Upadhyaya Grameen Kaushalya Yojana (DDU-GKY) 2014 Ministry of Rural Development - Objective: Skill rural poor youth (15-35) for sustainable employment.
- Scope: Residential training; placement in non-farm jobs.
- Application: Targets BPL families; 70% out-of-state placement. 		- Provisions: Free training, boarding; post-placement support for 6 months.
- Implementation: Project partners; aligns with NSDM. 		12 lakh youth trained; 8 lakh placed (2023-25); 2025 focus on green jobs.
Digital India Skill Initiative 2020 Ministry of Electronics and IT - Objective: Upskill 1 Cr youth in digital technologies; bridge digital divide.
- Scope: Training in AI, cybersecurity, data analytics; online/offline modes.
- Application: Targets unemployed youth; industry certifications. 		- Provisions: Free courses via platforms like Coursera, NASSCOM; internships.
- Implementation: Partnerships with IITs, private sector. 		50 lakh youth trained (2023-25); 30% in AI/ML; 2025 expansion to 2 Cr via NEP integration.
Entrepreneurship Schemes
Start-Up India 2016 Ministry of Commerce and Industry - Objective: Build ecosystem for youth-led innovation and startups.
- Scope: Recognition, funding, tax exemptions for startups.
- Application: Supports youth entrepreneurs in tech, social impact. 		- Provisions: Seed Fund Scheme, tax holiday for 3 years; IPR fast-track.
- Implementation: Incubators, angel tax exemption. 		1 lakh startups recognized; 1 Cr jobs created (2023-25); 2025 target: 1 lakh Cr funding.
Stand Up India 2016 Ministry of Finance - Objective: Promote entrepreneurship among SC/ST/women youth.
- Scope: Loans ₹10 lakh-₹1 Cr for greenfield enterprises.
- Application: Targets youth-led startups in manufacturing/services. 		- Provisions: Composite loans, handholding, credit guarantee.
- Implementation: Banks/SIDBI; training via hubs. 		80% beneficiaries women/SC/ST; 1.8 lakh enterprises funded (2025); 2024 expansion to digital ventures.
Mera Yuva Bharat (MY Bharat) 2023 Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports - Objective: Engage youth in nation-building via volunteering, leadership.
- Scope: Digital platform for opportunities in sports, culture, skills.
- Application: Connects youth with NYKS, NSS; promotes social engagement. 		- Provisions: Challenges, events, mentorship; integrates NYPF.
- Implementation: App-based; 2 Cr youth target. 		1 Cr users registered (2025); 5 lakh engaged in volunteering; 2025 Viksit Bharat Dialogue integrated.
Employment and Insurance Schemes
Employment-Linked Incentive (ELI) Scheme 2025 Ministry of Labour and Employment - Objective: Boost formal job creation; incentivize hiring of first-time workers.
- Scope: Covers 3.5 Cr jobs (2025-27); focuses on manufacturing, EPFO registrants.
- Application: Subsidies for employers/employees; promotes financial literacy. 		- Provisions: Part A: ₹15,000 one-month wage for first-timers; Part B: ₹3,000/month/employee for employers.
- Implementation: DBT via EPFO; Aadhaar-linked; valid Aug 2025-Jul 2027. 		₹99,446 Cr outlay; 50 lakh jobs targeted; 1.92 Cr first-timers covered (2025 pilot).
Pradhan Mantri Jeevan Jyoti Bima Yojana (PMJJBY) 2015 Ministry of Finance - Objective: Provide life insurance to youth/unorganized sector workers.
- Scope: ₹2 lakh cover for 18-50 years; annual premium ₹436.
- Application: Renewable; auto-debit from savings account. 		- Provisions: Natural/accidental death cover; claims via banks.
- Implementation: Linked to Jan Dhan accounts. 		15 Cr enrolled (2025); 5 lakh claims settled; 2024 digital renewal enhanced.
Pradhan Mantri Suraksha Bima Yojana (PMSBY) 2015 Ministry of Finance - Objective: Accident insurance for youth/families.
- Scope: ₹2 lakh accidental death/disability; premium ₹20/year (18-70 years).
- Application: Low-cost cover for vulnerable youth. 		- Provisions: Partial/full disability benefits; bank-linked.
- Implementation: Auto-enrollment via Aadhaar. 		35 Cr enrolled; 2 lakh claims (2023-25); 2025 integration with ELI for youth.
Code on Social Security (2020) 2020 Ministry of Labour and Employment - Objective: Consolidate labor laws for gig/unorganized youth workers.
- Scope: EPF, ESI, gratuity for 9 labor codes; gig worker welfare.
- Application: Protects youth in informal sectors. 		- Provisions: Social security fund for unorganized; maternity benefits extended.
- Implementation: Rules notified 2024; aligns with ELI. 		50 Cr workers covered; gig fund ₹10,000 Cr (2025); 2024 rules boost youth compliance.
Health and Other Schemes
Mental Healthcare Act (2017) 2017 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Protect rights of youth with mental illness; promote access to care.
- Scope: Decriminalizes suicide attempt; mandates treatment equality.
- Application: Addresses youth mental health crisis. 		- Provisions: Advance directives, nominated representatives; ban on ECT without consent.

Prime Minister Internship Scheme 2024 Ministry of Corporate Affairs - Objective: Provide 1-year internships to youth for skill-building.
- Scope: 1.25 lakh slots in top 500 companies; stipend ₹5,000/month + ₹6,000 one-time.

- Focus: Focus on regional job demands, apprenticeships. 		- Core Functions: Training centers, industry linkages, SSDM evaluations.
- Activities: IndiaSkills 2025 registrations; Northeast SSDM workshops (Apr 2021, ongoing).
- Structure: State nodal agencies; District Skill Committees. 		1.21 Cr apprentices enrolled (Mar 2025); 63 skills in IndiaSkills 2025; J&K launch across 20 districts (2025).
Allied Training and Employment Boards (e.g., State Employment Boards) Varies State Governments - Objective: Facilitate youth employment, apprenticeships, local skilling.
- Scope: Regional job fairs, vocational boards under NCS/SSDMS.
- Application: Supports MSDE schemes; integrates with NCS portals. 		- Core Functions: Job matching, counseling, training linkages.
- Activities: State-level job fairs; convergence with SSDMS for 2025 competitions.
- Structure: State labor/employment departments. 		50 lakh jobs via ELI (2025); integrated with NCS for 4M placements; regional SSDM expansions.
- All policies, acts, welfare schemes, etc in India for elderly -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
Constitutional Provisions
Article 41 (Directive Principles) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Directs state to provide public assistance in cases of old age, sickness, and disability.
- Scope: Non-justiciable; guides welfare policies for vulnerable groups including elderly.
- Application: Foundation for schemes like NSAP, IPOP; promotes right to dignified life. 		- Provisions: State to secure right to work, education, public assistance within economic capacity.
- Implementation: Influences NSAP pensions, NPHCE health care. 		Supports 2.5 Cr elderly via NSAP (2025); integrated into AVYAY for comprehensive elderly welfare.
Legislative Acts
Maintenance and Welfare of Parents and Senior Citizens Act 2007 (Amended 2019) Central Legislation - Objective: Ensure maintenance, welfare, and protection for parents/senior citizens.
- Scope: Covers abandonment, abuse; mandates family duty to provide care.
- Application: Establishes tribunals for claims; promotes old age homes. 		- Provisions: Maintenance up to ₹10,000/month (no upper limit post-2019); speedy tribunals (90 days); protection from eviction (Sections 4-23).
- Amendments (2019): Expands 'children' to include step/adoptive; removes cap on maintenance; adds homecare services. 		12% awareness among elderly (India Ageing Report 2023); 30,000+ cases resolved via tribunals (2023-25); 2025 focus on digital filing.
National Policies
National Policy for Senior Citizens 2011 (New Policy in Draft 2025) Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Ensure financial security, health, shelter for elderly; promote active ageing.
- Scope: Holistic welfare; focuses on 60+ population projected at 17.3 Cr by 2026.
- Application: Guides schemes like AVYAY, RVY; emphasizes intergenerational bonding. 		- Provisions: Universal pension, geriatric care, age-friendly environment; National Council for monitoring.
- Updates: Draft 2025 addresses silver economy, research; aligns with Viksit Bharat@2047. 		New policy under formulation (Jun 2025); covers 10.38 Cr elderly (2011 Census); 55% awareness of pensions (2023 Report).
Pension Schemes
Indira Gandhi National Old Age Pension Scheme (IGNOAPS) 2007 Ministry of Rural Development - Objective: Provide monthly pension to BPL elderly for basic needs.
- Scope: Non-contributory; targets 60+ BPL seniors.
- Application: Part of NSAP; direct benefit transfer via DBT. 		- Benefits: ₹200/month (60-79 yrs), ₹500/month (80+ yrs); state top-ups possible.
- Implementation: Aadhaar-linked; via banks/post offices. 		2.5 Cr beneficiaries (2025); ₹12,000 Cr disbursed (2023-25); reduced vulnerability by 10% (LASI data).
Pradhan Mantri Vaya Vandana Yojana (PMVVY) 2017 Ministry of Finance (LIC) - Objective: Assured pension for senior citizens' financial security.
- Scope: Annuity scheme for 60+; max ₹15 lakh investment.
- Application: Lump-sum purchase; monthly/quarterly payouts. 		- Benefits: 7.4% p.a. return; 10-year term; death benefit refunds purchase price.
- Implementation: Via LIC; extended to Mar 2023. 		₹2.1 lakh Cr mobilized; 3.2 Cr accounts (2025); 5 lakh claims settled.
Atal Pension Yojana (APY) 2015 Ministry of Finance (PFRDA) - Objective: Portable pension for unorganized sector, including elderly.
- Scope: Guaranteed ₹1,000-₹5,000/month at 60; for 18-40 yrs.
- Application: Voluntary contributions; govt. guarantee on returns. 		- Benefits: Fixed pension; spouse/nominee continuation; tax benefits under 80CCD.
- Implementation: Via banks/post offices; Aadhaar-linked. 		6.5 Cr subscribers (2025); ₹15,000 Cr corpus; 1 Cr new enrollments (2023-25).
Varishta Pension Bima Yojana (VPBY) 2003 (Revived 2014) Ministry of Finance (LIC) - Objective: Immediate annuity for senior citizens' income security.
- Scope: 60+; single premium for lifelong pension.
- Application: 9% guaranteed return; monthly/quarterly options. 		- Benefits: Annuity from ₹500-₹6,000/month; spouse continuation.
- Implementation: LIC-managed; open Aug 2014-Aug 2015, renewals ongoing. 		3.16 lakh annuitants; ₹6,095 Cr corpus (2023-25); 1 lakh renewals processed.
Annapurna Scheme 2000 Ministry of Rural Development - Objective: Food security for uncovered elderly BPL seniors.
- Scope: 65+ indigent not under IGNOAPS.
- Application: Complements NSAP; state-implemented. 		- Benefits: 10 kg free rice/month per beneficiary.
- Implementation: Via PDS; transferred to state plan 2002-03. 		22,000 beneficiaries (2025); 2.64 lakh MT rice distributed (2023-25); 12% awareness (2023 Report).
Health Schemes
National Programme for Health Care of the Elderly (NPHCE) 2010 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Specialized geriatric care via dedicated facilities.
- Scope: Preventive/promotive care; covers chronic diseases.
- Application: Outreach at PHCs/CHCs; aligns with NPOP. 		- Provisions: Geriatric clinics, OPD/IPD wards; training for staff (80% central funding).
- Implementation: 100 districts/year; 12 regional centers. 		₹1,500 Cr allocated (2023-25); 1 Cr elderly screened; 80% Anganwadis upgraded for ECCE integration.
Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (AB-PMJAY) 2018 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Cashless health coverage for poor, including elderly.
- Scope: ₹5 lakh/family/year; secondary/tertiary care.
- Application: Empanelled hospitals; extended to 70+ seniors (2024). 		- Benefits: Covers 1,949 procedures; no cap on family size.
- Implementation: Ayushman cards; subsumes SCHIS/RSBY. 		6 Cr seniors (70+) covered (2025); 3 Cr treatments for elderly; ₹5,118 Cr utilized (2024).
Senior Citizens Health Insurance Scheme (SCHIS) 2018 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Health insurance for uninsured 60-80 yrs seniors.
- Scope: Renewable to 90 yrs; covers hospitalization.
- Application: Subsumed under AB-PMJAY; low-premium policy. 		- Benefits: Annual coverage; free check-ups; daycare treatments.
- Implementation: Via insurers; Aadhaar-linked. 		Integrated into PMJAY (2023); 50 lakh elderly claims (2023-25); 70% compliance in formal sector.
Welfare Schemes
Rashtriya Vayoshri Yojana (RVY) 2017 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Assistive devices for BPL seniors with disabilities.
- Scope: Age-related infirmities; free aids via camps.
- Application: 325 districts; ALIMCO-implemented. 		- Provisions: Hearing aids, wheelchairs, dentures; full cost coverage.
- Implementation: Assessment/distribution camps; SCWF-funded. 		1.2 lakh beneficiaries (2023-25); 77 camps in 135 districts; ₹500 Cr devices distributed.
Integrated Programme for Older Persons (IPOP) 1992 (Revamped as IPSrC) Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Improve quality of life via basic amenities, active ageing.
- Scope: Shelter, food, health; supports NGOs/PRIs.
- Application: Capacity building; now AVYAY (2021). 		- Provisions: Grants up to ₹5 lakh/project; old age homes, day care.
- Implementation: 100% central funding; 1,000+ projects. 		₹100 Cr released (2023-25); 1.2 lakh rehabilitated; subsumed under AVYAY covering 14.3 Cr elderly (2021 proj.).
Atal Vayo Abhyuday Yojana (AVYAY) 2021 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Holistic elderly empowerment; subsumes IPOP/RVY.
- Scope: Financial security, health, active ageing; 10 developmental goals.
- Application: Umbrella for silver economy, research. 		- Components: Pension, geriatric care, intergenerational bonding; ₹2.6 lakh Cr gender budget integration.
- Implementation: State/UT coordination; NGOs/PRIs. 		₹3,437 Cr outlay (2025); 6 Cr families benefited; 2025 focus on accessibility.
Elder Line (14567) 2021 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Toll-free helpline for elderly grievances, support.
- Scope: Abuse, isolation, legal aid; 8 AM-8 PM daily.
- Application: Emotional, on-ground intervention; NISD-operated. 		- Services: Counseling, referrals, rescues; multilingual.
- Implementation: Call centers, field teams, NGOs. 		4 lakh calls handled (2023-25); 30,000 cases resolved; 2 lakh from Maharashtra alone.

Body/Agency Year Established Authority Purpose/Description Key Functions/Activities Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
National Level Bodies
National Council for Senior Citizens (NCSrC) 2012 Advisory Body (MoSJE) - Objective: Advise Central/State Governments on senior citizens' welfare and quality of life enhancement.
- Scope: Reconstituted from NCOP; focuses on policy, schemes, and demographic needs.
- Application: Reviews schemes like RVY, IPSrC; promotes active ageing. 		- Core Functions: Policy recommendations, scheme monitoring, grievance mechanisms.
- Activities: Meetings on NPSC draft, digital inclusion, intergenerational bonding.
- Structure: Chaired by Union Minister; includes experts, state reps. 		4th meeting chaired by Dr. Virendra Kumar (Jun 2025); discussed NPSC draft for 20% elderly by 2047; one meeting in last 3 years prior (2023).
Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment (MoSJE) 1985 Central Ministry - Objective: Promote social justice, welfare for marginalized including elderly.
- Scope: Nodal for senior citizens; oversees AVYAY, RVY, Elder Line.
- Application: Formulates policies, funds schemes; coordinates with states. 		- Core Functions: Policy-making, scheme implementation, NGO partnerships.
- Activities: AVYAY (umbrella scheme), NPHCE, IPOP; grievance redressal.
- Structure: Led by Dr. Virendra Kumar; includes DSJE, DSW. 		₹3,437 Cr for AVYAY (2025); 10.38 Cr elderly covered (2011 Census, proj. 17.3 Cr by 2026); RRTCs in 21 states.
Department of Social Justice and Empowerment (DSJE) 1985 Under MoSJE - Objective: Implement social welfare programs for disadvantaged groups including seniors.
- Scope: Handles elderly division; schemes like IPSrC, RVY.
- Application: Funds NGOs, monitors state action plans. 		- Core Functions: Scheme funding, capacity building, research.
- Activities: Grants to VOs, physiotherapy clinics, old age homes.
- Structure: Division for Senior Citizens; coordinates with NISD. 		₹100 Cr grants under IPSrC (2023-25); supports 1,000+ projects; subsumed into AVYAY (2021).
National Institute of Social Defence (NISD) 1961 Autonomous Body (MoSJE) - Objective: Human resource development in social defence including elderly welfare.
- Scope: Training, research on ageing, abuse prevention.
- Operation: Operates Elder Line; supports AVYAY. 		- Core Functions: Training programs, awareness, capacity building.
- Activities: TAPAS online courses on elderly care; 3-month de-addiction courses; physiotherapy clinics.
- Structure: HQ Delhi; focuses on drug abuse, elderly, transgenders. 		Shortlisted candidates for Batch I FY 2025-26; TAPAS courses on elderly care; 1M+ functionaries trained (cumulative).
Elder Line (National Helpline for Senior Citizens) 2021 MoSJE (Operated by NISD) - Objective: Provide support for elderly grievances, information, emotional aid.
- Scope: Toll-free 14567; covers abuse, isolation, legal/financial issues.
- Application: Pan-India; 8 AM-8 PM, multilingual. 		- Core Functions: Counseling, referrals, rescues; links to schemes.
- Activities: On-ground interventions, volunteer calls, feedback.
- Structure: Call centers, field teams, NGO partners. 		4 lakh calls handled (2023-25); 30,000 cases resolved; 2 lakh from Maharashtra; available 8 AM-8 PM daily.
Senior Citizens Welfare Fund (SCWF) 2015 Finance Act (Administered by MoSJE) - Objective: Fund welfare schemes using unclaimed deposits for BPL seniors.
- Scope: Sources from savings, PF, insurance; supports RVY, IPOP.
- Application: Promotes financial security, health, shelter. 		- Core Functions: Allocate funds for schemes; interest on deposits.
- Activities: Transfers unclaimed amounts; supports physiotherapy, homes.
- Structure: Secretariat in MoSJE; nodal for administration. 		₹6,095 Cr corpus (2023); 3.35% interest FY 2024-25; funds 1.2 lakh beneficiaries via RVY.
NGOs and Partnerships
HelpAge India 1978 NGO (Partners with MoSJE) - Objective: Improve disadvantaged elderly's quality of life via holistic care.
- Scope: Healthcare, advocacy, livelihood; operates in 26 states.
- Application: Mobile healthcare, digital literacy, abuse prevention. 		- Core Functions: Programs for active ageing, rights advocacy.
- Activities: AdvantAge60 campaign (2025); intergenerational reports; eye surgeries.
- Structure: 26 state offices; partners with govt for schemes. 		UN Population Award 2020; AdvantAge60 launch (Sep 2025); report on intergenerational dynamics (Jun 2025); 2400 villages reached.
Agewell Foundation 1999 NGO (Partners with MoSJE, NITI Aayog) - Objective: Empower elderly via research, advocacy, welfare services.
- Scope: Dignity, independence; focuses on abuse, health.
- Application: Community programs, policy inputs. 		- Core Functions: Awareness, capacity building; Solo Aging study.
- Activities: Share the Warmth winter aid; COVID impact studies.
- Structure: Associated with govt working groups. 		Solo Aging study (Oct 2024); winter relief 2025; contributes to NITI Aayog sub-group.
State and Local Level Bodies
State Councils for Senior Citizens Varies (e.g., 2012 onwards) State Governments (Under MoSJE Guidelines) - Objective: State-level advisory on elderly welfare, mirroring NCSrC.
- Scope: Policy implementation, scheme monitoring; e.g., Kerala Elderly Commission (2025).
- Application: Addresses local needs like abuse, health. 		- Core Functions: Recommendations, grievance redressal, state action plans.
- Activities: Intergenerational programs, digital inclusion.
- Structure: Chaired by state minister; includes experts, reps. 		Kerala first Elderly Commission (Mar 2025); 25+ states affiliated via AISCCON; ₹1 Cr annual budget in Kerala.
District-level Maintenance Tribunals (Under MWPSC Act) 2007 State Governments (MWPSC Act) - Objective: Adjudicate maintenance claims, protect from abandonment/abuse.
- Scope: One per sub-division; handles claims up to ₹10,000/month.
- Application: Quasi-judicial; speedy resolution (90 days). 		- Core Functions: Inquiries, orders for maintenance/property restoration.
- Activities: Conciliation, evidence collection; appeals to Appellate Tribunal.
- Structure: Presided by SDM/DM; no legal practitioners barred (HC rulings). 		30,000+ cases resolved (2023-25); SC quashed gift deeds for non-maintenance (Jan 2025); 700+ districts.
Regional Resource and Training Centres (RRTCs) 2010 MoSJE (Implemented by NGOs) - Objective: Provide technical support for aged care in assigned states.
- Scope: Training, inputs on programs; 21 RRTCs across India.
- Application: Capacity building for NGOs, PRIs. 		- Core Functions: Workshops, research, scheme implementation aid.
- Activities: Elderly care training, awareness generation.
- Structure: NGO-led; supervised by MoSJE. 		21 RRTCs operational (2025); trained 10,000+ functionaries; supports AVYAY outreach.
- All policies, acts, welfare schemes, etc in India for SC’s help -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
Constitutional Provisions
Article 14 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Ensures equality before the law and equal protection of laws for SCs.
- Scope: Prohibits arbitrary discrimination; basis for affirmative action.
- Application: Supports reservations, anti-discrimination measures. 		- Provisions: Equal treatment; enables special provisions for SCs under Article 15(4).
- Implementation: Enforced via courts; landmark cases like Indra Sawhney (1992). 		Applied in 2024 SC sub-classification verdict; 15% reservation upheld in jobs/education (2025).
Article 15(4) & 15(5) (Fundamental Rights) 1951 (1st Amendment) Constitution of India - Objective: Enables special provisions for SCs in education and employment.
- Scope: 15(4) for backward classes; 15(5) for private institutions.
- Application: Justifies reservations; promotes social justice. 		- Provisions: State can make provisions for advancement of SCs; excludes creamy layer.
- Implementation: Guides scholarships, quotas. 		Supports 15% seats in higher education; 2024 Supreme Court allows sub-classification within SCs.
Article 17 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Abolishes untouchability; prohibits its practice in any form.
- Scope: Criminalizes forced labor, discrimination against SCs.
- Application: Basis for anti-atrocities laws. 		- Provisions: Punishable offense; supports civil rights.
- Implementation: Enforced via PCR Act. 		Linked to 4.09 lakh SC atrocity cases (2014-2022); 2025 awareness drives intensified.
Article 46 (Directive Principles) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Promotes educational/economic interests of SCs; protects from social injustice.
- Scope: Guides state policy for weaker sections.
- Application: Influences SCSP, scholarships. 		- Provisions: State to promote welfare; non-justiciable but policy directive.
- Implementation: Basis for SC Sub-Plan. 		Supports ₹1.66 lakh Cr SC welfare allocation (2024-25); 74.74% utilized.
Legislative Acts
Protection of Civil Rights Act 1955 Central Legislation - Objective: Penalizes untouchability practices; enforces Article 17.
- Scope: Covers denial of access to temples, water sources.
- Application: Complements PoA Act. 		- Provisions: Imprisonment up to 6 months; fines (Sections 3-7).
- Implementation: State enforcement; cognizable offenses. 		Integrated with PoA Act; 2024 NCRB: 1,000+ cases; low conviction (20%).
Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes (Prevention of Atrocities) Act (PoA Act) 1989 (Amended 2015, 2018) Central Legislation - Objective: Prevent atrocities against SCs; ensures speedy justice.
- Scope: Criminalizes discrimination, violence; special courts.
- Application: Protects from social boycott, land grabs. 		- Provisions: Atrocities punishable up to life; relief/rehab (Sections 14-18).
- Amendments: 2015 removed anticipatory bail; 2018 added new offenses. 		51,656 cases in 2022 (NCRB); 61.7% acquittal rate; 2025: 1,000 fast-track courts.
Employment of Manual Scavengers and Construction of Dry Latrines (Prohibition) Act 1993 Central Legislation - Objective: Ban manual scavenging; dismantle dry latrines.
- Scope: Prohibits employment in hazardous cleaning.
- Application: Precursor to 2013 Act. 		- Provisions: 1-year imprisonment; fines (Sections 3-5).
- Implementation: State surveys; rehab funds. 		Subsumed in 2013 Act; 2025: 1,000 deaths prevented via NAMASTE.
Prohibition of Employment as Manual Scavengers and their Rehabilitation Act 2013 Central Legislation - Objective: Eradicate manual scavenging; rehabilitate workers.
- Scope: Bans hazardous cleaning; survey/rehab.
- Application: Targets SC-dominated occupation. 		- Provisions: Rehab cash (₹40,000), education, housing (Sections 9-13).
- Implementation: NSKFDC; surveys every 5 years. 		58,000 identified (2023); ₹1,200 Cr rehab funds; integrated with NAMASTE (2025).
Development Plans
Scheduled Castes Sub-Plan (SCSP) 1979 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Channel proportional funds (16.6%) for SC development.
- Scope: Education, health, housing; monitored via DAPSC.
- Application: Ensures targeted schemes. 		- Provisions: 15-20% budget earmark; non-divertible.
- Implementation: Annual action plans; NITI Aayog oversight. 		₹1.66 lakh Cr allocated (2024-25); 74.74% utilized (lowest in decade); revamp proposed.
Scholarship Schemes
Post-Matric Scholarships for SC Students 1977 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Financial aid for higher education of SC students.
- Scope: Post-Class 10; covers tuition, maintenance.
- Application
- All bodies, agencies, etc in India for SC’s help -
Body/Agency Year Established Authority Purpose/Description Key Functions/Activities Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
National Level Bodies
National Commission for Scheduled Castes (NCSC) 2004 Constitutional Body (Article 338) - Objective: Safeguard rights of SCs; monitor constitutional safeguards against exploitation.
- Scope: Promotes social, educational, economic, cultural interests; investigates grievances.
- Application: Apex advisory body; powers of civil court for inquiries. 		- Core Functions: Investigate complaints, recommend policies, monitor SCSP implementation.
- Activities: Fact-finding, awareness camps, annual reports; GHAR portal for missing SCs.
- Structure: Chairperson (Cabinet Minister rank), Vice-Chairperson, 4 members. 		Chairperson: Shri Kishor Makwana (2025); 1 lakh complaints (Apr-Dec 2024); 18,954 resolved; new 'checklist' for rejecting invalid complaints (Oct 2025); Ambedkar Jayanti event (Apr 2025).
National Commission for Safai Karamcharis (NCSK) 1994 Non-Statutory Body (MoSJE) - Objective: Promote welfare of safai karamcharis (mostly SCs); monitor manual scavenging eradication.
- Scope: Recommends policies for sanitation workers' upliftment; investigates grievances.
- Application: Focuses on health, safety, wages; implements MS Act 2013. 		- Core Functions: Grievance redressal, policy recommendations, monitoring schemes.
- Activities: Suo motu inquiries, awareness programs, rehab support.
- Structure: Chairperson (MoS rank), Vice-Chairperson, 5 members. 		Tenure extended to Mar 2028 (Feb 2025); ₹50.91 Cr budget (2025-28); monitors NAMASTE; 58,000 scavengers identified/rehabbed (2023).
Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment (MoSJE) 1985 Central Ministry - Objective: Promote social justice for SCs, OBCs, etc.; implement welfare schemes.
- Scope: Oversees NCSC, NSFDC, scholarships; focuses on education, economic empowerment.
- Application: Nodal for SC development; coordinates with states. 		- Core Functions: Policy formulation, funding, monitoring SCSP.
- Activities: AVYAY integration, PM-DAKSH training, sub-classification guidelines.
- Structure: Led by Dr. Virendra Kumar; DSJE handles SC division. 		₹1.66 lakh Cr for SC welfare (2024-25); 74.74% utilized; new RRTCs in 21 states (2025); PM-DAKSH trained 10,000+ SCs.
Department of Social Justice and Empowerment (DSJE) 1985 Under MoSJE - Objective: Implement SC welfare programs; focus on education, health, finance.
- Scope: Manages scholarships, SCSP; supports NSFDC, NSKFDC.
- -: Funds NGOs, monitors state corporations. 		- Core Functions: Scheme execution, grants, research.
- Activities: Post-Matric scholarships, Pre-Matric aid; SC sub-plan monitoring.
- Structure: SC Development Bureau; coordinates with NCSC. 		₹100 Cr grants (2023-25); 50 lakh SC students aided via scholarships; 2025 focus on digital inclusion.
National Safai Karamchari Finance and Development Corporation (NSKFDC) 1997 Govt. Undertaking (MoSJE) - Objective: Socio-economic upliftment of safai karamcharis (SCs) via finance, skills.
- Scope: Loans for self-employment; targets manual scavengers' dependents.
- Application: Implements rehab under MS Act 2013. 		- Core Functions: Concessional loans, skill training, marketing support.
- Activities: Mahila Adhikarita Yojana, Swachhata Nidhi; via SCAs, banks.
- Structure: Not-for-profit; Board with govt, SC reps. 		₹1,200 Cr rehab funds (2023); 58,000 scavengers identified; Shilp Samagam Mela (Jun 2025); 1 lakh+ beneficiaries.
National Scheduled Castes Finance and Development Corporation (NSFDC) 1989 Govt. Undertaking (MoSJE) - Objective: Promote economic empowerment of SCs below 2x poverty line.
- Scope: Loans for income generation; skill development.
- Application: Via SCAs, banks; focuses on self-employment. 		- Core Functions: Term loans, micro-finance, EDP training.
- Activities: Margin money loans up to ₹20 lakh; PM-DAKSH integration.
- Structure: Not-for-profit; ₹1,500 Cr share capital. 		₹1.66 lakh Cr disbursed (cumulative); 10 lakh+ SCs empowered (2023-25); 2025: 1 Cr trainees via PM-DAKSH.
Dr. Ambedkar Foundation 1992 Autonomous Body (MoSJE) - Objective: Promote Ambedkar's ideals; support SC education, health, culture.
- Scope: Scholarships, medical aid, memorials; research on social justice.
- Application: Empowers SC/ST youth; runs DAIC. 		- Core Functions: Awards, fellowships, publications.
- Activities: Dr. Ambedkar National Merit Award; medical aid scheme.
- Structure: Chairman (MoSJE); HQ at DAIC, New Delhi. 		Merit Awards to 500+ SC students (2025); ₹75 lakh/chair for 31 universities (2025); Ambedkar Jayanti events (Apr 2025).

Scheduled Caste Development Corporations (State Level) Varies (e.g., 1971 Haryana) State Governments (under MoSJE CSS) - Objective: Economic development of SCs via loans, subsidies.
- Scope: 27 corporations; implements NSFDC/NSKFDC schemes.
- Application: Targets self-employment; 49:51 central-state funding. 		- Core Functions: Identify beneficiaries, sponsor loans, provide margin money.
- Activities: Skill training, marketing; tie-ups with banks.
- Structure: State-owned; e.g., HSFDC (51% state, 49% GOI). 		₹1,200 Cr disbursed via 27 SCDCs (2023-25); 5 lakh SC families assisted; 2025 revamp for viability.

- Scope: Scholarships, pensions, atrocity prevention; surveys.
- Application: Local coordination for PoA Act, PM-DAKSH. 		- Core Functions: Beneficiary verification, scheme sanctions, awareness.
- Activities: Camps for scholarships, rehab for manual scavengers.
- Structure: Headed by District Social Welfare Officer; 700+ districts. 		50 lakh scholarships sanctioned (2023-25); 1 lakh atrocity cases handled; 2025 digital portals for applications.
Other Implementing Agencies
Venture Capital Fund for Scheduled Castes (VCFSC) 2015 MoSJE (Managed by NSFDC) - Objective: Promote SC entrepreneurship via equity/debt funding.
- Scope: Startups by SCs; up to 25% equity participation.
- Application: Supports innovation; ₹200 Cr corpus. 		- Core Functions: Fund viable projects; incubation support.
