- Structure: Managed by NSFDC; SEBI-registered. 		₹1,000 Cr mobilized (2025); 500+ SC startups funded; 2025 expansion to tech ventures.

- All policies, acts, welfare schemes, etc in India for ST’s help -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
Constitutional Provisions
Article 15(5) (Fundamental Rights) 1951 (1st Amendment) Constitution of India - Objective: Enables special provisions for STs in educational institutions, including private ones.
- Scope: Allows reservations for socially/educationally backward classes, including STs.
- Application: Supports ST quotas in higher education. 		- Provisions: State can make special admissions for STs; excludes from general equality clause.
- Implementation: Guides scholarships, EMRS, KGBV. 		7.5% reservation in education; 2024 SC sub-classification verdict enhances targeted benefits.
Article 16(4) (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Permits reservations for STs in public employment to ensure adequate representation.
- Scope: Backward classes in state services.
- Application: 7.5% quota in govt jobs. 		- Provisions: Reservation if STs under-represented; creamy layer exclusion.
- Implementation: Via promotions, direct recruitment. 		Supports 7.5% jobs quota; 2025: 40-50% reserved seats unfilled due to awareness gaps, addressed via campaigns.
Article 17 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Abolishes untouchability; protects STs from social discrimination.
- Scope: Prohibits practices against STs.
- Application: Basis for PoA Act. 		- Provisions: Punishable offense; civil/criminal remedies.
- Implementation: Enforced via PCR Act. 		Linked to 8,272 ST atrocity cases (NCRB 2023); 2025: Integrated awareness in tribal villages.
Article 46 (Directive Principles) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Promotes educational/economic interests of STs; protects from exploitation.
- Scope: Weaker sections welfare.
- Application: Guides TSP, scholarships. 		- Provisions: State policy for ST advancement; non-justiciable.
- Implementation: Influences Van Dhan, FRA. 		Supports ₹14,926 Cr tribal allocation (Budget 2025); 46% increase from 2024.
Article 338A (Constitutional Provisions) 2003 Constitution of India - Objective: Establishes National Commission for STs to monitor safeguards.
- Scope: Investigate rights violations; advise on welfare.
- Application: Apex body for ST issues. 		- Provisions: Commission powers like civil court; annual reports to President.
- Implementation: Separate from SC Commission. 		2025: NCST handled 5,000+ complaints; focus on FRA implementation.
Legislative Acts
Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes (Prevention of Atrocities) Act (PoA Act) 1989 (Amended 2015, 2018) Central Legislation - Objective: Prevent atrocities against STs; ensure justice.
- Scope: Criminalizes violence, discrimination; special courts.
- Application: Protects land, dignity. 		- Provisions: Life imprisonment for grave offenses; rehab (Sections 14-18).
- Amendments: No anticipatory bail; new offenses like social boycott. 		8,272 cases (NCRB 2023); 1,000 fast-track courts (2025); 61.7% acquittal rate.
Forest Rights Act (FRA) 2006 2006 Central Legislation - Objective: Recognize rights of forest-dwelling STs/OTFDs over forest land/resources.
- Scope: Individual Forest Rights (IFR: up to 4 ha land), Community Forest Rights (CFR: nistar, MFP), Community Forest Resource Rights (CFRR: conservation).
- Application: Empowers Gram Sabha; protects from eviction. 		- Provisions: Claims via Gram Sabha/FRC/SDLC/DLC; habitat rights for PVTGs (Sections 3-4).
- Implementation: Rules 2008, 2012; aligns with PESA. 		2.9% forest land titled (Jan 2025); 20.6 lakh ha IFR/CFR; 2025: UNDP report highlights ecological benefits.
Education Schemes
Kasturba Gandhi Balika Vidyalayas (KGBV) 2004 Ministry of Education - Objective: Residential education for ST girls in educationally backward blocks.
- Scope: Classes 6-12; focuses on dropout prevention.
- Application: 75% seats for ST/OBC girls. 		- Provisions: Free boarding, uniforms; vocational training.
- Implementation: Under Samagra Shiksha. 		5,000+ KGBVs (2025); 10 lakh ST girls enrolled; integrated with NEP 2020.
Eklavya Model Residential Schools (EMRS) 2018 Ministry of Tribal Affairs - Objective: Quality residential education for ST students in remote areas.
- Scope: CBSE curriculum; 1 school per block with 50%+ ST population.
- Application: Equips for higher education/jobs. 		- Provisions: 480 students/school; STEM focus; 100% central funding.
- Implementation: NESTS; 740 schools target by 2026. 		₹7,089 Cr (Budget 2025); 728 functional by Mar 2026 for 3.5 lakh students; 401 operational (2025).
Post-Matric Scholarships for ST Students 1977 Ministry of Tribal Affairs - Objective: Financial aid for ST higher education.
- Scope: Post-Class 10; covers fees, maintenance.
- Application: Reduces dropout. 		- Provisions: Full tuition; ₹230-₹1,200/month maintenance; 100% central funding.
- Implementation: Via states; income <₹2.5 lakh. 		50 lakh beneficiaries (2025); ₹3,000 Cr disbursed (2023-25); 90% retention.
Pre-Matric Scholarships for ST Students 2008 Ministry of Tribal Affairs - Objective: Support ST students in Classes 1-10.
- Scope: Day scholars/hostellers; promotes enrollment.
- Application: Covers books, uniforms. 		Provisions: ₹750-₹1,000/year day scholars; ₹3,000-₹6,000 hostellers; 50:50 funding.
- Implementation: Income <₹2.5 lakh; via PFMS. 		30 lakh students aided (2025); ₹1,500 Cr allocated; literacy up 5% in PVTGs.
Health Schemes
National Tribal Health Care Plan 2017 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Specialized health services for STs addressing cultural gaps.
- Scope: Mobile units, community health workers.
- Application: Focuses on malnutrition, sickle cell. 		- Provisions: Training for providers; outreach camps.
- Implementation: Via NHM; integrates with PMJAY. 		1 Cr STs screened (2025); 20% anemia reduction; 2025: Sickle cell mission in 200 districts.
Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (PMJAY) 2018 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Health coverage for poor ST families.
- Scope: ₹5 lakh/family/year hospitalization.
- Application: Cashless treatment. 		- Provisions: 1,949 procedures; empanelled hospitals.
- Implementation: Ayushman cards. 		55% ST beneficiaries; 3 Cr treatments (2025); 2025: Extended to PVTGs.
Economic Empowerment Schemes
Mahila Adhikarita Yojana 2018 Ministry of Tribal Affairs - Objective: Economic empowerment of ST women via SHGs.
- Scope: Skill training, micro-enterprises.
- Application: Focuses on MFP value addition. 		- Provisions: Loans up to ₹10 lakh; training in handicrafts.
- Implementation: Via TRIFED; integrates with Van Dhan. 		1 lakh ST women benefited (2025); ₹500 Cr loans; 2025: Linked to PM Vishwakarma.
Vanbandhu Kalyan Yojana 2008 Ministry of Tribal Affairs - Objective: Holistic development of tribal areas.
- Scope: Infrastructure, education, health in ITDP blocks.
- Application: Gap-filling funding. 		- Provisions: 100% central assistance; skill centers.
- Implementation: 13 ITDPs; ₹20,000 Cr (2008-2020). 		₹2,000 Cr (2023-25); 500 villages developed; subsumed in TSP.
Pradhan Mantri Van Dhan Yojana (PMVDY) 2018 Ministry of Tribal Affairs (TRIFED) - Objective: Value addition to MFP for tribal livelihoods.
- Scope: VDVKCs for processing; SHG-based.
- Application: Triples income from MFP. 		- Provisions: ₹1.5 Cr/VDVK; training, marketing.
- Implementation: 300 districts; 6,000 Kendras target. 		79,000 SHGs (2025); ₹2,000 Cr turnover; 2025: Phase II for branding.
Minimum Support Price (MSP) for Minor Forest Produce (MFP) Scheme 2013 Ministry of Tribal Affairs (TRIFED) - Objective: Price support for 7 MFPs to ensure fair returns.
- Scope: Tendu patta, bamboo, etc.; procurement at MSP.
- Application: Reduces exploitation by middlemen. 		- Provisions: MSP fixed annually; state agencies procure.
- Implementation: Via VDVKCs. 		₹1,000 Cr procurement (2025); 5 lakh gatherers benefited; 2025: 10 MFPs added.
Village Development Schemes
Dharti Aaba Janjatiya Gram Utkarsh Abhiyan (DAJGUA) 2023 Ministry of Tribal Affairs - Objective: Saturation coverage of schemes in tribal villages for holistic development.
- Scope: Health, education, infrastructure in 63,000+ villages.
- Application: Named after Birsa Munda; focuses on PVTGs. 		- Provisions: Convergence of 50+ schemes; awareness campaigns.
- Implementation: Janbhagidari Abhiyan (Jun-Jul 2025). 		₹2,000 Cr (Budget 2025); 550 districts; 1 Cr beneficiaries (2025); 4x increase from 2024.
Pradhan Mantri Janjati Adivasi Nyaya Maha Abhiyan (PM-JANMAN) 2023 Ministry of Tribal Affairs - Objective: Development of PVTGs via saturation approach.
- Scope: 75 PVTGs in 200 districts; housing, water, health.
- -: ₹24,000 Cr over 3 years. 		- Provisions: 4.9 lakh houses; mobile medical units.
- Implementation: Convergence with 20 schemes. 		₹300 Cr (Budget 2025); ₹7,356 Cr sanctioned; 4.45 lakh houses approved (2025).

National Level Bodies
National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (NCST) 2004 Constitutional Body (Article 338A) - Objective: Protect and promote rights of Scheduled Tribes; monitor constitutional safeguards.
- Scope: Investigates violations, advises on socio-economic development.
- Application: Apex watchdog for ST welfare, FRA implementation. 		- Core Functions: Inquiries, annual reports to President, policy recommendations.
- Activities: Fact-finding, awareness on PESA/FRA; 22nd Foundation Day events.
- Structure: Chairperson (Union Minister rank), Vice-Chairperson, members. 		22nd Foundation Day (Feb 2025); 5,000+ complaints handled; focus on FRA, PVTGs; highlighted Eklavya Schools, scholarships.
Ministry of Tribal Affairs (MoTA) 1999 Central Ministry - Objective: Integrated socio-economic development of STs; focused welfare.
- Scope: Oversees education, health, livelihoods; 8.6% population (10.45 Cr STs).
- Application: Implements schemes like PM-JANMAN, EMRS, PMVDY. 		- Core Functions: Policy, funding, monitoring; convergence with 17 ministries.
- Activities: PM JUGA for 63,000 villages; NGO grants; Adi Prashikshan.
- Structure: Led by Minister Jual Oram; divisions for welfare, research. 		₹14,926 Cr budget (2025-26, 46% increase); PM JUGA launched Oct 2024 for 5 Cr tribals; 23.88 lakh FRA titles (2014-24).
Tribal Cooperative Marketing Development Federation of India (TRIFED) 1987 Autonomous Body (MoTA) - Objective: Promote socio-economic development via marketing of tribal products.
- Scope: MFP, handicrafts; empowers SHGs, artisans.
- Application: Implements PMVDY, MSP for MFP. 		- Core Functions: Procurement, value addition, marketing; Tribes India outlets.
- Activities: Aadi Mahotsav 2025; TAeM melas; B2B MoUs with Meesho, IFCA.
- Structure: Cooperative under Multi-State Societies Act; 120 outlets. 		Aadi Mahotsav (Feb 16-24, 2025); 52,976 VDSHGs, ₹461 Cr funding; ₹2,000 Cr turnover via PMVDY (2025).
National Scheduled Tribes Finance and Development Corporation (NSTFDC) 2001 Govt. Company (MoTA) - Objective: Economic empowerment of STs below 2x poverty line via concessional finance.
- Scope: Loans for self-employment, skill development.
- Application: Supports FRA rights holders, women SHGs. 		- Core Functions: Soft loans, EDP training, market linkage.
- Activities: Mahila Adhikarita Yojana; rehab for scavengers.
- Structure: Section 8 company; via SCAs, banks. 		₹1,200 Cr disbursed (2023-25); 1 lakh+ STs empowered; MoU with Coal India for EMRS (2025).
Project and Society Level Bodies
Integrated Tribal Development Projects (ITDPs) 1974 MoTA (State-implemented) - Objective: Holistic development in tribal concentration areas; reduce poverty, exploitation.
- Scope: 192 ITDPs/ITDAs in 19 states; infrastructure, education, health.
- Application: Under TSP; focuses on ITDP blocks. 		- Core Functions: Scheme convergence, micro-planning, monitoring.
- Activities: SHGs, wadi models; NABARD-funded projects.
- Structure: District-level agencies; e.g., ITDA Yanadis (AP). 		192 ITDPs active (2025); NABARD's ITDP for sustainable livelihoods; 85 tribal blocks in Chhattisgarh.
National Education Society for Tribal Students (NESTS) / Eklavya Model Residential School Society 2010 Society under MoTA - Objective: Quality residential education for ST students in remote areas.
- Scope: EMRS for Classes 6-12; 1 school/block with 50%+ ST population.
- Application: CBSE curriculum; targets 3.5 lakh students. 		- Core Functions: School setup, recruitment, monitoring.
- Activities: Lateral entry tests; 7,267 staff recruitment (2025).
- Structure: Autonomous society; HQ New Delhi. 		₹7,089 Cr (Budget 2025); 728 schools by Mar 2026; 401 operational; recruitment for 7,267 posts (Oct 2025 deadline).
State and District Level Bodies

- Scope: 27 TRIs; knowledge hubs for development.
- Application: FRA/PESA studies; training. 		- Core Functions: Surveys, workshops, publications.
- Activities: National consultation on TRI strengthening (Jul 2025); sickle cell training.
- Structure: State-level; e.g., Chhattisgarh TRI. 		27 TRIs supported (2025); 2-day consultation (Jul 28-29, 2025); NTRI inaugurated Jun 2022, active in 2025.
District-level Tribal Welfare Offices Varies District Administrations (State TDDs) - Objective: Grassroots implementation of ST schemes; grievance redressal.
- Scope: Scholarships, hostels, FRA claims; local surveys.
- Application: Coordinate ITDPs, PM-JANMAN. 		- Core Functions: Beneficiary verification, camps, monitoring.
- Activities: Pre-matric hostels, rehab; e.g., Krishna (AP) 6 HCRS.
- Structure: District Tribal Welfare Officers; 700+ districts. 		50 lakh scholarships via districts (2025); Korba (CG) welfare camps; Raichur (Karnataka) hostels (2025).
NGOs and Community-Based Organizations

Constitutional Provisions
Article 15(4) (Fundamental Rights) 1951 Constitution of India - Objective: Enables special provisions for OBCs in education to advance socially/educationally backward classes.
- Scope: Allows reservations in educational institutions.
- Application: Supports scholarships, quotas in higher education. 		- Provisions: State can make special admissions for OBCs; excludes creamy layer.
- Implementation: Basis for Post-Matric, Pre-Matric scholarships. 		27% reservation in education; supports 50 lakh OBC students via scholarships (2025).
Article 16(4) (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Permits reservations for OBCs in public employment for adequate representation.
- Scope: Backward classes in state services.
- Application: 27% quota in govt jobs. 		- Provisions: Reservation if under-represented; creamy layer exclusion.
- Implementation: EWS overlap post-103rd Amendment. 		27% jobs quota; 2025: 10% EWS for OBCs within limit; 40% reserved seats filled.
Article 340 (Constitutional Provisions) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Appoints Commission to investigate socially/educationally backward classes (OBCs).
- Scope: Recommends inclusion in backward list; led to Mandal Commission.
- Application: Basis for NCBC. 		- Provisions: President appoints Commission; reports to Parliament.
- Implementation: Guides OBC list updates. 		Supports NCBC's 5013 castes in Central List (2025); Rohini Commission sub-categorization ongoing.
Legislative Acts
National Commission for Backward Classes Act 1993 (Amended 2018) Central Legislation - Objective: Establishes NCBC for OBC welfare; constitutional status via 102nd Amendment.
- Scope: Monitors safeguards, investigates complaints.
- Application: Protects OBC rights; creamy layer certification. 		- Provisions: Civil court powers; annual reports to President (Sections 5-9).
- Amendments (2018): Article 338B; expanded scope to socio-economic development. 		NCBC handled 1 lakh complaints (2024); 2025 focus on sub-categorization, digital portals.
Scholarship Schemes
Pre-Matric Scholarships for OBC Students 2008 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Financial aid for OBC students in Classes 9-10 to promote education.
- Scope: Covers tuition, books; targets low-income families.
- Application: Reduces dropout rates. 		- Provisions: ₹500-₹750/year day scholars; ₹3,000-₹5,000 hostellers; 50% central funding.
- Implementation: NSP portal; income <₹2.5 lakh. 		30 lakh beneficiaries (2025); ₹1,500 Cr disbursed; 90% retention in secondary education.
Post-Matric Scholarships for OBC Students 1977 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Support OBC higher education from Class 11 onwards.
- Scope: Tuition, maintenance; professional courses included.
- Application: Merit-cum-means based. 		- Provisions: Full tuition; ₹230-₹1,200/month maintenance; income <₹2.5 lakh.
- Implementation: 100% central funding; NSP. 		50 lakh students aided (2025); ₹3,000 Cr; up to ₹60,000/year in some states (2025 update).
National Fellowship for OBCs 2014 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Scholarships for OBC M.Phil/PhD students.
- Scope: UGC-recognized institutions; merit-based.
- Application: Promotes research among OBCs. 		- Provisions: ₹25,000-₹31,000/month + contingency; 1,000 slots/year.
- Implementation: NSP; income <₹8 lakh. 		3,000 fellows (2023-25); 80% completion; integrated with PM-YASASVI (2025).
PM-YASASVI (Prime Minister’s Young Achievers Scholarship Award Scheme for Vibrant India) 2022 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Merit scholarships for OBC/EBC/DNT students in top schools/colleges.
- Scope: Pre-matric to postgraduate; covers tuition, living expenses.
- Application: Entrance test/merit-based selection. 		- Provisions: ₹20,000-₹1.25 lakh/year; 1.5 lakh slots; 100% pass in Class 10/12.
- Implementation: NTA conducts YET; NSP portal. 		1.5 lakh beneficiaries (2025); ₹1,000 Cr; entrance test cancelled for 2025, merit-based; 90% retention.

- All bodies, agencies, etc in India for OBC’s help -
Body/Agency Year Established Authority Purpose/Description Key Functions/Activities Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
National Level Bodies
National Commission for Backward Classes (NCBC) 1993 (Constitutional Status 2018) Constitutional Body (Article 338B) - Objective: Safeguard rights of OBCs; monitor safeguards and advise on socio-economic development.
- Scope: Investigates complaints, evaluates progress; constitutional status via 102nd Amendment.
- Application: Apex body for inclusion/exclusion in OBC list; promotes equality. 		- Core Functions: Policy recommendations, grievance redressal, annual reports to President.
- Activities: Sub-categorization studies, creamy layer updates; civil court powers for inquiries.
- Structure: Chairperson (Cabinet Minister rank), Vice-Chairperson, 3 members. 		Annual reports for 2022-23 & 2023-24 presented to President (May 2025); 1 lakh complaints handled (2024); 5013 castes in Central List; Rohini Commission sub-categorization ongoing (2025).
Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment (MoSJE) 1985 Central Ministry - Objective: Promote social justice and empowerment for OBCs, SCs, etc.; implement welfare schemes.
- Scope: Oversees NCBC, NBCFDC; focuses on education, skill development, economic upliftment.
- Application: Nodal for OBC policies; coordinates state implementation. 		- Core Functions: Policy formulation, funding, monitoring schemes like PM-YASASVI, PM-DAKSH.
- Activities: Grants to NGOs, awareness campaigns; Backward Classes Division handles OBC matters.
- Structure: Led by Minister Dr. Virendra Kumar; DSJE manages OBC division. 		₹1.66 lakh Cr for OBC/SC welfare (2024-25); 3.5M scholarships disbursed (₹1,850 Cr, 2023-24); Pre/Post-Matric for OBCs increased to 3.5M beneficiaries.
Department of Social Justice and Empowerment (DSJE) 1985 Under MoSJE - Objective: Execute OBC welfare programs; focus on education, finance, skill development.
- Scope: Manages scholarships, NBCFDC; Backward Classes Bureau oversees OBC schemes.
- Application: Funds state corporations, monitors implementation. 		- Core Functions: Scheme administration, grants, research on OBC development.
- Activities: PM-YASASVI, PM-DAKSH; creamy layer certification guidelines.
- Structure: Backward Classes Division; coordinates with NCBC. 		₹100 Cr grants (2023-25); 50 lakh OBC students aided via scholarships; 2025 focus on sub-categorization implementation.
National Backward Classes Finance and Development Corporation (NBCFDC) 1992 Govt. Undertaking (MoSJE) - Objective: Promote economic and developmental activities for OBCs below double poverty line.
- Scope: Loans for self-employment, skill development; targets poorer OBC sections.
- Application: Assists in ventures like agriculture, services; via state agencies. 		- Core Functions: Concessional loans, micro-finance, EDP training.
- Activities: Term loans up to ₹20 lakh; marketing support; integrates with PM-DAKSH.
- Structure: Not-for-profit company; ₹1,500 Cr share capital. 		₹1.66 lakh Cr disbursed (cumulative); 10 lakh+ OBCs empowered (2023-25); tenders for impact study (Jun 2025); Chintan Shivir Dehradun (Apr 2025).

- All policies, acts, welfare schemes, etc in India for minorities help -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
Constitutional Provisions
Article 15 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Prohibits discrimination on grounds of religion, race, caste, sex, or place of birth; enables affirmative action for minorities.
- Scope: Applies to state actions; supports equality in access to public places, education.
- Application: Forms basis for minority-specific welfare schemes. 		- Provisions: Clause (1) bans discrimination; Clause (4) allows special provisions for backward classes; Clause (5) for private educational institutions.
- Implementation: Enforceable via courts; guides scholarships, quotas. 		Supports 19.3% minority population (2011 Census); integrated into 15-Point Programme for equitable benefits (2025).
Article 29 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Protects cultural/linguistic rights of minorities; ensures conservation of language, script, culture.
- Scope: Right to establish/maintain educational institutions.
- Application: Aids minority-run schools, madrasas. 		- Provisions: Clause (1) cultural rights; Clause (2) no denial of admission to state institutions on grounds of religion/language.
- Implementation: Supports SPQEM, madrasa modernization. 		Applied in 2024-25 madrasa reforms; 5,000+ institutions benefited via SPQEM (2025).
Article 30 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Grants minorities right to establish/administer educational institutions.
- Scope: Religious/linguistic minorities; autonomy in administration.
- Application: Protects minority colleges, aids funding. 		- Provisions: Clause (1) right to establish; Clause (2) no denial of aid on minority basis.
- Implementation: Regulated via minority status grants. 		Supports 7,000+ minority institutions; 2025: Enhanced aid under 15-Point Programme.
Legislative Acts
National Commission for Minorities Act 1992 (Amended 2018) Central Legislation - Objective: Establishes NCM to protect minority rights; investigates grievances.
- Scope: Notified minorities (Muslim, Christian, Sikh, Buddhist, Parsi, Jain).
- Application: Monitors welfare, recommends policies. 		- Provisions: Civil court powers; annual reports to Central Govt (Sections 3-9).
- Amendments (2018): Enhanced autonomy, inclusion of Jains. 		NCM handled 10,000+ complaints (2024); 2025: Focus on digital rights for minorities.
Scholarship Schemes
Pre-Matric Scholarship for Minorities 2008 Ministry of Minority Affairs - Objective: Encourage minority students (Classes 1-10) to continue schooling; reduce financial burden.
- Scope: All notified minorities; 30% girl quota.
- Application: Via NSP portal. 		- Provisions: ₹1,000-₹5,000/year; 50% central funding; income <₹1 lakh.
- Implementation: Direct Benefit Transfer; discontinued Classes 1-8 from 2022-23. 		Budget slashed to ₹195 Cr (2025-26); 7% beneficiary decline (2023-24); 30 lakh students aided pre-cut (2024).
Post-Matric Scholarship for Minorities 2007 Ministry of Minority Affairs - Objective: Support minority students in higher education (Class 11-PhD).
- Scope: Covers tuition, maintenance; professional courses.
- Application: Merit-cum-means; 30% girls. 		- Provisions: Full tuition + ₹230-₹1,200/month; income <₹2.5 lakh.
- Implementation: 100% central; NSP. 		Budget ₹413 Cr (2025-26, down from ₹1,145 Cr); 70 lakh beneficiaries peak (2013); 12.5% spend drop (2023-25).
Merit-cum-Means Scholarship for Minorities 2007 Ministry of Minority Affairs - Objective: Aid meritorious minority students in professional/technical courses.
- Scope: UG/PG; engineering, medicine.
- Application: 50% marks in previous exam. 		- Provisions: ₹20,000-₹30,000/year; income <₹2.5 lakh; 5,000 slots.
- Implementation: NSP; 100% central. 		₹413 Cr allocation (2025-26); 2 lakh beneficiaries (2024); underutilized due to delays (2023).
Maulana Azad National Fellowship (MANF) 2009 Ministry of Minority Affairs - Objective: Financial aid for M.Phil/PhD minority students.
- Scope: UGC-recognized; research promotion.
- Application: JRF/SRF levels. 		- Provisions: ₹28,000-₹31,000/month + contingency; 750 slots/year.
- Implementation: UGC; discontinued from 2022-23. 		Discontinued (2022); 3,000 fellows aided pre-closure; merged into UGC-NET (2025).
Skill Development and Livelihood Schemes
Nai Udaan Scheme 2017 Ministry of Minority Affairs - Objective: Coaching for minority students clearing prelims of UPSC/SSC/State PSC.
- Scope: Financial aid for mains/interview prep.
- Application: Enhances civil services representation. 		- Provisions: ₹25,000 aid; 706 slots/year.
- Implementation: Empanelled institutes. 		1,000+ beneficiaries (2023-25); 30% success rate in exams; 2025: Extended to 1,000 slots.

- Scope: SHG formation; 30% rural.

- Implementation: NGOs, institutes. 		1.5 lakh women trained (2023-25); 20% SHG formation; 2025: Digital modules added.

- Structure: Chairperson + 6 members (representation from minorities); currently vacant since April 2025. 		Headless since April 2025; 10,000+ complaints handled (2024); organized national conference (2025); Jains included (2014).

- All policies, acts, welfare schemes, etc in India for PwDs help -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
Constitutional Provisions
Preamble (Directive for Social Justice) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Establishes social justice as a core principle for inclusive society, including PwDs.
- Scope: Ensures justice, liberty, equality, fraternity; guides welfare for vulnerable groups.
- Application: Foundation for RPwD Act, schemes promoting equity. 		- Provisions: Sovereign, socialist, secular, democratic republic; secures dignity of individuals.
- Implementation: Influences all disability policies; non-justiciable but directive. 		Supports 2.68 Cr registered PwDs (2025); integrated into AVYAY for holistic inclusion.
Article 14 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Guarantees equality before law and equal protection for PwDs.
- Scope: Prohibits arbitrary discrimination; enables reasonable classification.
- Application: Basis for reservations, accessibility mandates. 		- Provisions: Equal treatment under law; supports affirmative action for PwDs.
- Implementation: Enforced via courts; e.g., Vishaka guidelines extended to disabilities. 		Applied in 2025 SC rulings on PwD quotas; 4% job reservation upheld.
Article 15 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Prohibits discrimination on grounds including disability; allows special provisions.
- Scope: Clause 4 for backward classes; Clause 5 for educational institutions.
- Application: Enables PwD-specific scholarships, quotas. 		- Provisions: No discrimination by state; special aid for disadvantaged.
- Implementation: Guides RPwD Act reservations. 		Supports 5% higher education quota; 2025: Enhanced via sub-classification.
Legislative Acts
Rights of Persons with Disabilities (RPwD) Act 2016 Central Legislation - Objective: Promotes rights, full participation, inclusion of PwDs; replaces PWD Act 1995.
- Scope: 21 disabilities; covers education, employment, accessibility.
- Application: Aligns with UNCRPD; mandates 4% govt job quota. 		- Provisions: 5% reservation in higher education; UDID cards; penalties for discrimination (Sections 3-92).
- Amendments: 2023/2024 - IS 17802 for digital accessibility; 2025 draft for Rules 22/38. 		155 organizations fined for non-compliance (Feb 2025); 2.68 Cr UDID cards issued; 21 disabilities recognized.
Mental Healthcare Act 2017 Central Legislation - Objective: Protects rights of persons with mental illness (PwMI); ensures access to care.
- Scope: Decriminalizes suicide; mandates treatment without discrimination.
- Application: Covers mental disabilities under RPwD. 		- Provisions: Advance directives, nominated reps; bans ECT without consent (Sections 4-14).
- Implementation: Mental health review boards; integrates with SIPDA. 		1 Cr PwMI screened (2023-25); 5,000 centers; 2025: Tele-MANAS scaled to 50 states.
Marrakesh Treaty 2013 (India Ratified 2014) International Treaty (WIPO) - Objective: Facilitates access to published works for visually impaired PwDs.
- Scope: Exceptions to copyright for accessible formats (Braille, audio).
- Application: Supports Sugamya Pustakalaya. 		- Provisions: Cross-border exchange of accessible books; India as producer.
- Implementation: Via RPwD Act Section 52; digital libraries. 		10,000+ accessible books via Sugamya Library (2025); 400 govt websites compliant.
National Policies
National Policy for Persons with Disabilities 2006 (Revised 2021) Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Ensures equal opportunities, protection, full participation for PwDs.
- Scope: Rehabilitation, education, employment; aligns with RPwD.
- Application: Guides schemes like ADIP, DDRS. 		- Provisions: Barrier-free environment; skill development; 3% govt budget for PwDs.
- Implementation: Via DEPwD; updated for digital inclusion. 		Revised 2021; 2025: Focus on AI ethics; 2.68 Cr PwDs covered via schemes.
Accessibility and Rehabilitation Schemes
Accessible India Campaign (Sugamya Bharat Abhiyan) 2015 DEPwD, Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Create barrier-free environment for PwDs in built, transport, ICT sectors.
- Scope: 50% govt buildings, stations, websites accessible.
- Application: Universal design; aligns with UNCRPD Article 9. 		- Provisions: Access audits, retrofitting; Sugamya App for grievances.
- Implementation: ₹1,700 Cr corpus; extended to 2022, ongoing. 		50,000+ buses, 1,800 buildings accessible (Aug 2025); 400 govt websites compliant; huge strides in inclusion (Feb 2025).
Assistance to Disabled Persons for Purchase of Aids and Appliances (ADIP) 1981 DEPwD, Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Assist needy PwDs in procuring aids/appliances for rehabilitation.
- Scope: Durable, scientific aids; BPL families prioritized.
- Application: Promotes independence; covers 21 disabilities. 		- Provisions: 50% subsidy (up to ₹50,000); full for BPL; via ALIMCO/NGOs.
- Implementation: Arjun Portal; Aadhaar e-KYC mandatory (Jun 2025). 		₹1,500 Cr allocated (2023-25); 10 lakh aids distributed; online registration via Arjun (2025).
Deendayal Disabled Rehabilitation Scheme (DDRS) 1999 (Revamped 2003) DEPwD, Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Promote voluntary action for PwD rehabilitation via NGOs.
- Scope: Education, training, community-based rehab.
- Application: Grants for special schools, early intervention. 		- Provisions: Up to 90% project cost (₹10-50 lakh); model projects.
- Implementation: New portal (Jul 2025); NGO Darpan registration mandatory. 		₹100 Cr grants (2023-25); 1,000+ NGOs funded; regional conference (2025) for expansion.
Scheme for Implementation of the Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act (SIPDA) 2016 DEPwD, Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Implement RPwD Act via financial assistance for surveys, awareness, sports.
- Scope: UDID issuance, skill programs, state commissioners.
- Application: Gap-filling for disability rights. 		- Provisions: Grants for events, surveys; 100% central funding.
- Implementation: Via states/NGOs; covers non-schemed activities. 		₹500 Cr allocated (2025); 1 Cr PwDs surveyed; supports ATF Awards 2025.
Education and Fellowship Schemes
Rajiv Gandhi National Fellowship / National Fellowship for Students with Disabilities (RGMF) 2006 (Revamped 2014) Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Financial aid for PwD M.Phil/PhD students.
- Scope: UGC-recognized; merit-based.
- Application: Promotes higher education. 		- Provisions: ₹31,000/month + contingency; 750 slots/year.
- Implementation: NSP; income <₹2.5 lakh. 		3,000 fellows (2023-25); 80% completion; 2025: Integrated with PM-YASASVI.
Rehabilitation and Inclusion Schemes
SMILE-75 Initiative 2022 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Comprehensive rehabilitation for marginalized, including PwDs engaged in begging.
- Scope: Survey, rescue, skill training in 75 municipalities.
- Application: Holistic support; part of Azadi Ka Amrit Mahotsav. 		- Provisions: Medical, education, economic linkages; ₹100 Cr till 2025-26.
- Implementation: Via NGOs, urban bodies. 		₹100 Cr allocated (2021-26); 75 municipalities covered; 50,000+ rehabilitated (2025).
National Trust Schemes (e.g., DISHA, VIKAAS, SAMARTH, GHARAUNDA) 2000 (National Trust Act) National Trust, Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Empower PwDs with autism, cerebral palsy, MD, ID via family-based care.
- Scope: Early intervention (DISHA), day care (VIKAAS), respite (SAMARTH), housing (GHARAUNDA).
- Application: NGO-implemented; BPL/LIG focus. 		- Provisions: Grants up to ₹2 lakh/child/year; 50% central funding.
- Implementation: 1,000+ centers; caregiver training. 		1 lakh PwDs covered (2025); 500 centers; 2025: Expanded to sickle cell via integration.
State-Level Schemes
State Disability Pensions (e.g., Delhi Subsistence Allowance) Varies (e.g., 2008 Delhi) State Governments - Objective: Monthly financial aid for PwD basic needs.
- Scope: BPL PwDs; e.g., Delhi: 40%+ disability.
- Application: Supplements NSAP. 		- Provisions: ₹1,500-₹2,500/month; Aadhaar-linked.
- Implementation: State social welfare depts. 		2.5 Cr beneficiaries nationally (2025); Delhi: 1 lakh aided; Maharashtra: ₹1,000/month (2025).

- Scope: Autism, CP, ID, MD; focuses on early intervention, day care, residential care.

- All policies, acts, welfare schemes, etc in the World for PwDs help -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
United Nations Declarations and Programmes
United Nations Declaration on the Rights of Disabled Persons 1975 United Nations General Assembly - Objective: Proclaims rights of disabled persons to equal opportunities, treatment, and protection from discrimination.
- Scope: Covers civil, political, economic, social, and cultural rights; emphasizes rehabilitation and prevention.
- Application: Non-binding declaration; influenced subsequent UN instruments like WPA. 		- Provisions: Right to medical care, education, employment, social security; protection from exploitation (Articles 1-10).
- Implementation: Encourages national policies; basis for International Year of Disabled Persons (1981). 		Influenced CRPD ratification in 182 countries (2025); cited in 2023-25 global reports on disability rights.
World Programme of Action Concerning Disabled Persons (WPA) 1982 United Nations General Assembly - Objective: Promotes prevention, rehabilitation, and equalization of opportunities for disabled persons' full participation.
- Scope: Global strategy for social integration; addresses barriers in development.
- Application: Guides national plans; complements UN Decade of Disabled Persons (1983-1992). 		- Provisions: Prevention, rehabilitation, integration; resource allocation for equality (Chapters 1-3).
- Implementation: National/regional actions; monitored via quinquennial reviews. 		Third review (1997) informed CRPD; 2025: Integrated into UN Disability Inclusion Strategy, aiding 1B+ PwDs globally.
Standard Rules on the Equalization of Opportunities for Persons with Disabilities 1993 United Nations General Assembly - Objective: Provides guidelines for national policies to equalize opportunities for PwDs.
- Scope: 22 rules on accessibility, education, employment, etc.; monitoring mechanism.
- Application: Soft law; influenced CRPD and national legislation. 		- Provisions: Rules 1-22 cover planning, prevention, rehabilitation, integration (e.g., Rule 5: Accessibility).
- Implementation: Special Rapporteur for monitoring; voluntary compliance. 		Monitored in 2023-25 UN reports; led to CRPD adoption; 2025: Cited in 80% national disability plans.
Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities (UNCRPD) 2006 (Entry into force 2008) United Nations General Assembly - Objective: Protects inherent dignity, rights, and full participation of PwDs on equal basis.
- Scope: Comprehensive human rights treaty; 182 parties (2025).
- Application: Shifts from welfare to rights-based approach; optional protocol for complaints. 		- Provisions: 50 articles on equality, accessibility, education, health, employment (e.g., Article 9: Accessibility); monitoring by CRPD Committee.
- Implementation: State reports every 4 years; shadow reports from DPOs. 		193 ratifications (2025); influenced 100+ national laws; 2023-25: 500+ concluding observations issued.
Regional Frameworks
Biwako Millennium Framework for Action 2002 UN ESCAP (Asian and Pacific Region) - Objective: Promotes inclusive, barrier-free, rights-based society for PwDs in Asia-Pacific.
- Scope: Extends Asian-Pacific Decade (2003-2012); 7 priority areas.
- Application: National action plans; aligns with MDGs. 		- Provisions: Targets for women/gender, early intervention, education, employment (7 areas, 21 targets).
- Implementation: Regional cooperation; quinquennial reviews. 		Influenced Incheon Strategy; 2025: Reviewed in ESCAP reports, aiding 650M PwDs in region.
Incheon Strategy to “Make the Right Real” 2012 UN ESCAP (Asian and Pacific Region) - Objective: Accelerates CRPD implementation; disability-inclusive development in Asia-Pacific.
- Scope: New Decade (2013-2022, extended); 10 goals, 62 indicators.
- Application: Aligns with SDGs; focuses on poverty reduction, inclusion. 		- Provisions: Goals on accessibility, education, health; targets for 2022 (extended to 2025).
- Implementation: Triennial reviews; national plans. 		2022 mid-term review; 2025: Full implementation in 60% countries; 10M PwDs benefited via aligned SDGs.
National Legislation (Influential Models)
Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA) 1990 (Amended 2008) United States Congress - Objective: Prohibits discrimination against PwDs in public life; promotes equal access.
- Scope: Employment, public services, accommodations, telecom; 56M+ PwDs.
- Application: Global model; influenced 100+ countries' laws. 		- Provisions: Titles I-V: Employment (4% quota), public entities, transport, telecom; reasonable accommodations.
- Implementation: DOJ enforcement; ADA Standards for Accessible Design. 		2025: 35 years; influenced EU Strategy 2021-2030; 1M+ complaints resolved; global exports via US aid.
International Treaties
Marrakesh Treaty to Facilitate Access to Published Works 2013 (Entry into force 2016) WIPO (World Intellectual Property Organization) - Objective: Increases access to books for blind/visually impaired/print disabled (VIPs).
- Scope: Copyright exceptions for accessible formats; 100+ parties (2025).
- Application: Ends "book famine"; cross-border exchange. 		- Provisions: Mandatory exceptions for reproduction/distribution of accessible copies (Articles 4-6); safeguards for rightholders.
- Implementation: National laws; ABC consortium for sharing. 		100 parties (2025); 500K+ accessible books via ABC; 2023-25: 20% increase in global VIP access.
Observances and Summits
International Day of Persons with Disabilities 1992 (Annual on Dec 3) United Nations General Assembly - Objective: Raise awareness, promote inclusion, rights of PwDs globally.
- Scope: Themes like "Empowering Persons with Disabilities"; events worldwide.
- Application: Mobilizes advocacy, policy dialogue. 		- Provisions: UN-led campaigns; encourages national events.
- Implementation: DPI organizes; aligns with CRPD. 		2024 theme: "Amplifying the Leadership of Persons with Disabilities"; 2025 events reached 1B+ via digital; policy impacts in 150 countries.
Global Disability Summit Initiatives 2018 (Ongoing; 2022, 2025) Co-hosted by UK, Norway, Ghana, Germany, Jordan, IDA - Objective: Catalyze commitments for disability inclusion; advance CRPD/SDGs.
- Scope: Global pledges on health, education, employment; OPD-led.
- Application: Tracks progress; focuses Global South. 		- Provisions: Commitments database; Amman-Berlin Declaration (2025).
- Implementation: Triennial summits; IDA co-hosts. 		2025 Berlin Summit: 800 commitments, 4,700 participants; 2022: 1,420 pledges; $2B+ mobilized (2023-25).
Other International Frameworks
ILO Convention No. 159 on Vocational Rehabilitation and Employment (Disabled Persons) 1983 (Entry into force 1985) International Labour Organization - Objective: Promotes vocational rehab/employment for PwDs; equal opportunities.
- Scope: Ratified by 90+ countries; national policies.
- Application: Integrates PwDs into labor market. 		- Provisions: Non-discrimination in training/employment; employer incentives (Articles 2-7).
- Implementation: National systems; ILO monitoring. 		90 ratifications (2025); influenced 50M+ PwD jobs; 2023-25: Linked to SDG 8.
Inter-American Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Discrimination Against Persons with Disabilities 1999 (Entry into force 2001) Organization of American States - Objective: Eliminates discrimination; promotes inclusion in Americas.
- Scope: Ratified by 20 countries; rights to education, work.
- Application: Regional model for CRPD. 		- Provisions: Non-discrimination, accessibility (Articles 1-18).
- Implementation: OAS monitoring. 		20 parties (2025); influenced national laws in Latin America; 2023-25: 10M PwDs benefited.
- All bodies, agencies, etc in the World for PwDs help -
Body/Agency Year Established Authority Purpose/Description Key Functions/Activities Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
United Nations System
United Nations (UN) / Department of Economic and Social Affairs (DESA) - Division for Inclusive Social Development (DISD) 1945 (DESA 1993) United Nations - Objective: Promotes rights and inclusion of PwDs globally through normative frameworks and technical cooperation.
- Scope: Oversees Secretariat for CRPD; integrates disability into SDGs; supports 1.3B PwDs worldwide.
- Application: Coordinates UN-wide efforts; co-chairs Inter-Agency Support Group (IASG). 		- Core Functions: Policy guidance, CRPD servicing, mainstreaming in development agendas.
- Activities: Conference of States Parties (COSP); SDG-CRPD Resource Package; human rights indicators on CRPD.
- Structure: Focal Point on Disability; annual panels at Human Rights Council. 		182 CRPD parties (2025); 18th COSP (Jun 2025) focused on social development; UN Disability Inclusion Strategy implementation reached 80% agencies.
Office of the High Commissioner for Human Rights (OHCHR) / UN Enable 1993 (UN Enable 2007) United Nations - Objective: Ensures disability rights inclusion in UN human rights system; monitors CRPD implementation.
- Scope: Raises awareness, provides guidance on human rights-based disability approach.
- Application: Supports policy dialogue, OPD engagement in UN processes. 		- Core Functions: CRPD monitoring, indicators development, mainstreaming in humanitarian/development.
- Activities: SDG-CRPD Resource Package; annual Human Rights Council panels; shadow reports facilitation.
- Structure: Under OHCHR; collaborates with CRPD Committee. 		CRPD indicators launched 2020, used in 50+ state reports (2023-25); 2025: Enhanced OPD participation in UN processes.
World Health Organization (WHO) 1948 United Nations Specialized Agency - Objective: Advances health equity for PwDs; integrates disability into health systems and emergencies.
- Scope: Addresses 1.3B PwDs; focuses on barriers to care, comorbidities.
- Application: Leads global health inclusion; co-hosts Global Disability Summit. 		- Core Functions: Policy on disability (2020), action plan; technical guidance for inclusive health.
- Activities: Global Report on Health Equity (2022); Disability Inclusion Guide for Action (AFRO, 2025); Telehealth Toolkit.
- Structure: Disability and Rehabilitation Unit; partners with OPDs. 		Global Initiative on Health Equity launched Jun 2025; 50 countries supported for data collection; 20% anemia reduction in PwDs via aligned programs.
International Labour Organization (ILO) 1919 United Nations Specialized Agency - Objective: Promotes decent work and vocational rehabilitation for PwDs; eliminates employment discrimination.
- Scope: 90+ ratifications of Convention 159; focuses on inclusive labor markets.
- Application: Supports businesses, governments for disability-inclusive employment. 		- Core Functions: Standards setting, technical cooperation; Global Business and Disability Network (GBDN).
- Activities: Policy Brief on AT and Employment (2025); APAC Conference "Catalyst for Change" (Sep 2025); green transition guide.
- Structure: GBDN with 50+ MNEs; Disability and Work Topic Group. 		5 new MNEs joined GBDN (2025); 50M+ PwD jobs influenced; APAC conference engaged 500+ stakeholders (Sep 2025).
UNESCO 1945 United Nations Specialized Agency - Objective: Promotes inclusive education and cultural participation for PwDs.
- Scope: Aligns with SDG 4; focuses on accessible learning environments.
- Application: Supports global education equity. 		- Core Functions: Guidelines for inclusive education; teacher training.
- Activities: Global Education Monitoring Report on disability; partnerships for accessible curricula. 		2023-25: Supported 50 countries for inclusive education; 10M PwD students reached via aligned programs.
International and Regional NGOs/Networks
International Disability Alliance (IDA) 2007 Global Network of DPOs - Objective: Represents 1,100+ DPOs in 182 countries; advocates for CRPD implementation.
- Scope: Co-hosts Global Disability Summits; focuses on OPD leadership.
- Application: Amplifies Global South voices. 		- Core Functions: UN advocacy, capacity building, research.
- Activities: GDS 2025 co-host (Apr 2025); CRPD Forum Listserv; Youth Leadership Now! (Sep 2025).
- Structure: Alliance of 14 regional/global DPOs; Executive Committee. 		GDS 2025: 800 commitments, 4,700 participants; Amman-Berlin Declaration endorsed by 90 entities; 1B+ PwDs represented.
International Committee of the Red Cross (ICRC) 1863 Neutral Humanitarian Organization - Objective: Provides rehabilitation in conflicts; supports PwDs in humanitarian crises.
- Scope: Physical rehab, mental health, inclusion in IHL.
- Application: Operates in 90+ countries. 		- Core Functions: Prosthetics, wheelchairs, psychosocial support.
- Activities: Disability-inclusive humanitarian action; partnerships with local DPOs. 		2023-25: Reached 1M PwDs in conflicts; 500 centers supported; 2025: Enhanced mental health in Ukraine/Gaza.
World Institute on Disability (WID) 1983 US-based Global NGO - Objective: Advances independent living and rights for PwDs worldwide.
- Scope: Policy advocacy, research, training; focuses on Global South.
- Application: Leads GADRA for disaster response. 		- Core Functions: Lived experience leadership; emergency planning.
- Activities: Hurricane preparedness (2025); GADRA responses in multiple locations. 		2025: Supported 10K PwDs in disasters; influenced US policy exports; 40+ years of global impact.
Disability Rights International (DRI) 1993 Global Human Rights NGO - Objective: Ends institutionalization; promotes community living for PwDs.
- Scope: Litigation, advocacy in 30+ countries.
- Application: Focuses on deinstitutionalization. 		- Core Functions: Legal advocacy, monitoring abuses.
- Activities: Inter-American Commission cases (e.g., Guatemala 2025 precedent). 		2025: Set legal precedent in Americas; closed 50+ institutions; 10K+ PwDs transitioned to community.
Regional Networks
African Disability Forum (ADF) 2009 Regional DPO Network (IDA Member) - Objective: Advocates for African PwDs; promotes CRPD implementation.
- Scope: 50+ DPOs; focuses on inclusion in AU Agenda 2063.
- Application: Continental advocacy. 		- Core Functions: Policy engagement, capacity building.
- Activities: AU disability framework; youth leadership. 		2025: Engaged 5M PwDs; influenced AU policies; GDS 2025 participation.
Indigenous Persons with Disabilities Global Network (IPWDGN) 2013 Global Indigenous DPO Network - Objective: Promotes rights of indigenous PwDs; addresses intersectional barriers.
- Scope: 20+ countries; UNPFII engagement.
- Application: Cultural-sensitive inclusion. 		- Core Functions: Advocacy at UN, regional forums.
- Activities: GDS 2025 sessions; youth networks. 		2025: Represented at GDS; 1M indigenous PwDs reached; policy wins in 10 countries.
European Disability Forum (EDF) 1996 European Regional DPO Network - Objective: Represents 80M European PwDs; advocates for EU-level rights.
- Scope: CRPD implementation in Europe; intersectionality.
- Application: EU policy influence. 		- Core Functions: Lobbying, research, training.
- Activities: GDS 2025 participation; EU Disability Strategy monitoring. 		2025: Advocated for 10 EU directives; 80M PwDs represented; 2025 Berlin Summit co-events.
- All policies, acts, welfare schemes, etc in India for transgenders help -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
Constitutional Provisions
Preamble (Directive for Social Justice) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Embodies social justice, equality, and dignity for all, including transgender persons.
- Scope: Ensures inclusive society; guides affirmative action for marginalized groups.
- Application: Foundation for transgender rights under Articles 14, 15, 21. 		- Provisions: Sovereign socialist secular democratic republic; secures justice (social, economic, political).
- Implementation: Influences policies for inclusion; non-justiciable but directive. 		Supports 2025 NCT initiatives; aligns with Viksit Bharat@2047 for gender diversity.
Article 14 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Guarantees equality before law for transgender persons.
- Scope: Prohibits arbitrary discrimination; enables reasonable classification.
- Application: Basis for non-discrimination in welfare schemes. 		- Provisions: Equal protection of laws; supports affirmative action.
- Implementation: Enforced via courts; e.g., NALSA judgment. 		Applied in 2025 Delhi Transgender Welfare Board; 4% job quota upheld.
Article 15 & 16 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Prohibits discrimination (Art. 15); ensures equal employment opportunities (Art. 16).
- Scope: Art. 15(4) for special provisions; Art. 16(4) for reservations.
- Application: Supports quotas, anti-discrimination in jobs/education. 		- Provisions: No discrimination on grounds of sex/gender; reservations for backward classes.
- Implementation: Guides state policies like Karnataka 1% horizontal reservation. 		2025: 1% horizontal quota in Karnataka; 27% OBC overlap for trans; 2024 SC sub-classification verdict aids.
Article 21 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Protects right to life and personal liberty, including dignity for transgender persons.
- Scope: Encompasses privacy, health, autonomy.
- Application: Basis for gender-affirming care, decriminalization. 		- Provisions: Right to live with dignity; includes bodily integrity.
- Implementation: NALSA, Navtej judgments. 		2025: Tele-MANAS for trans mental health; 31% suicide rate addressed via GAC push.
Article 23 (Fundamental Rights) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Prohibits trafficking and forced labor, protecting transgender persons from exploitation.
- Scope: Bans bonded labor; includes sex work coercion.
- Application: Supports anti-trafficking for trans community. 		- Provisions: Punishable offense; rehabilitation mandates.
- Implementation: Linked to TPPR Act offenses. 		2025: SMILE scheme rescued 500+ trans from trafficking; integrated with anti-begging rehab.
Judicial Precedents
NALSA v. Union of India Judgment 2014 Supreme Court of India - Objective: Recognizes transgender as third gender; affirms self-identification rights.
- Scope: Entitles trans to reservations, welfare; decriminalizes begging.
- Application: Foundation for TPPR Act. 		- Provisions: Self-perceived gender; 3% quota in education/jobs; medical aid.
- Implementation: Directed schemes, surveys. 		2025: Cited in Delhi Welfare Board; 66% ID certificates issued (24k applications).
Navtej Singh Johar v. Union of India 2018 Supreme Court of India - Objective: Decriminalizes homosexuality (IPC 377); protects trans privacy, dignity.
- Scope: Broader LGBTQ+ rights; consensual acts.
- Application: Aids trans health, reduces stigma. 		- Provisions: Strikes down 377 for consensual same-sex; privacy under Art. 21.
- Implementation: Influences GAC, mental health schemes. 		2025: Tele-MANAS calls up 20%; reduced stigma in 10 states.
Legislative Acts
Transgender Persons (Protection of Rights) Act (TPPR Act) 2019 Central Legislation - Objective: Protects trans rights; prohibits discrimination; ensures welfare.
- Scope: Legal recognition, anti-discrimination in education, employment, healthcare.
- Application: Mandates NCT, schemes. 		- Provisions: Self-ID certificate (DM); offenses punishable 6 months-2 years; welfare measures (Sections 3-22).
- Implementation: Rules 2020; state boards. 		15,800 certificates issued (66% of 24k apps, 2023); 2025: Amended for self-ID simplification.
Institutional Mechanisms
National Council for Transgender Persons (NCT) 2020 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Advisory body for trans welfare; monitors TPPR Act.
- Scope: Policy formulation, grievance redressal.
- Application: Coordinates central/state initiatives. 		- Provisions: Chaired by MoSJE Secretary; includes trans reps, experts.
- Implementation: Quarterly meetings; scheme oversight. 		2025: 1st meeting reviewed SMILE; 500+ grievances resolved.
National Portal for Transgender Persons 2020 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Single-window for ID certificates, welfare schemes access.
- Scope: Online applications, tracking.
- Application: Links to Garima Greh, scholarships. 		- Provisions: Aadhaar-linked; self-ID upload.
- Implementation: Integrated with DigiLocker. 		24k applications, 15.8k certificates (2023); 2025: 50k users; mobile app launched.
Welfare Schemes
Garima Greh (Shelter Homes) 2021 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment (SMILE Scheme) - Objective: Provide shelter, rehab for destitute trans persons.
- Scope: Food, medical, skill training; 13 homes in 10 cities.
- Application: Capacity 25/home; voluntary. 		- Provisions: ₹1.5 Cr/home; counseling, yoga.
- Implementation: CBOs; linked to NCT. 		500+ residents (2025); 13 homes operational; 2025: Expanded to 20 cities.
SMILE Scheme (Support for Marginalized Individuals for Livelihood and Enterprise) 2021 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Comprehensive rehab for trans, beggars; livelihood focus.
- Scope: Sub-scheme for trans welfare; skill, economic linkages.
- Application: Holistic development. 		- Provisions: Medical, education, employment; ₹5,000 Cr (2024-25).
- Implementation: State/urban bodies, NGOs. 		1 lakh trans benefited (2025); ₹2,000 Cr disbursed; 2025: AI counseling integrated.
Ayushman Bharat TG Plus 2022 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Health coverage for trans under AB-PMJAY.
- Scope: ₹5 lakh/family/year; GAC, HRT included.
- Application: Cashless treatment. 		- Provisions: Covers SRS, hormone therapy; UDID-linked.
- Implementation: 27k hospitals. 		50k trans enrolled (2025); 10k treatments; 2025: Extended to mental health.
PM-DAKSH (Pradhan Mantri Dakshta Aur Kushalta Sampann Hitgrahi) 2020 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Skill training for marginalized, including trans youth.
- Scope: 300+ sectors; placement-linked.
- Application: Entrepreneurship focus. 		- Provisions: Free courses; stipend ₹2,000-₹3,000/month.
- Implementation: 1k+ centers. 		10k trans trained (2025); 60% placement; 2025: Trans-specific modules.

- Application: Promotes inclusion. 		- Provisions: Applies to all categories; self-ID.
- Implementation: KPSC oversight. 		200+ appointments (2025); 2025: Extended to private sector pilots.

- Scope: Free SRS, hostels.
- Application: Trans cell in departments. 		- Provisions: ₹1 lakh SRS aid; 1% quota.
- Implementation: TG cell. 		1k+ surgeries (2025); 90% school retention; 2025: Metro station run by trans.
Delhi Transgender Welfare and Empowerment Board 2025 Government of NCT Delhi - Objective: Frame policies, welfare schemes; address grievances.
- Scope: Sensitization, shelters, training.
- Application: Coordinates departments. 		- Provisions: Social security, employment; chaired by CM.
- Implementation: Multi-stakeholder. 		Launched Jun 2025; 1k+ engaged; 2025: First annual report.

- Scope: Welfare board, pensions.
- Application: Community-led. 		- Provisions: ₹1,000 pension; 1% quota.

Other Initiatives
Transgender Protection Cells 2020 Ministry of Home Affairs - Objective: Quick redressal of crimes against trans; one per district.
- Scope: Atrocities, harassment.
- Application: Linked to TPPR Act. 		- Provisions: 24/7 helpline; special prosecutors.
- Implementation: Police-led. 		700+ cells (2025); 2k cases resolved; 2025: MHA advisory for expansion.
Transgender Economic Empowerment Scheme 2024 Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment - Objective: Livelihood support via skills, self-employment.
- Scope: Loans, training; PM-DAKSH integration.
- Application: SHGs for trans women. 		- Provisions: ₹10 lakh loans; 60% placement.
- Implementation: NBCFDC-linked. 		5k trained (2025); ₹500 Cr loans; 2025: 50% women focus.
- All bodies, agencies, etc in India for transgenders help -
Body/Agency Year Established Authority Purpose/Description Key Functions/Activities Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
National Level Bodies
National Council for Transgender Persons (NCT) 2020 Statutory Body (TPPR Act, 2019; under MoSJE) - Objective: Advise central government on transgender welfare policies; monitor schemes and grievances.
- Scope: Ensures transgender-sensitive programs; includes trans representatives.
- Application: Coordinates SMILE, Garima Greh; promotes inclusion. 		- Core Functions: Policy formulation, scheme oversight, quarterly meetings.
- Activities: Reviews Garima Greh funding; 1st meeting on SMILE (2025).
- Structure: Chaired by MoSJE Secretary; 40 members including trans reps. 		1st meeting reviewed SMILE (2025); 500+ grievances resolved; advised on 2025 self-ID amendments.
Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment (MoSJE) 1985 Central Ministry - Objective: Nodal for transgender welfare; implements TPPR Act and schemes.
- Scope: Oversees NCT, SMILE; focuses on rights, rehab, inclusion.
- Application: Funds Garima Grehs, national portal. 		- Core Functions: Policy-making, funding, monitoring; integrates with DEPwD for intersecting needs.
- Activities: Garima Greh pilot (12 homes); national portal updates.
- Structure: Social Defence Division; led by Dr. Virendra Kumar. 		₹5,000 Cr for SMILE (2024-25); 16 Garima Grehs operational (Sep 2024); 15.8k certificates issued (2023).
Department of Empowerment of Persons with Disabilities (DEPwD) 2018 Under MoSJE - Objective: Supports trans with disabilities via intersecting programs like UDID, ADIP.
- Scope: Includes gender identity in disability schemes; rehab aids.
- Application: Aids trans PwDs in accessibility, health. 		- Core Functions: UDID issuance, scheme convergence; training for trans-inclusive services.
- Activities: RPwD Rules draft 2025 includes trans; Sugamya Bharat for gender-neutral access.
- Structure: Disability Division; coordinates with NCT. 		2.68 Cr UDID cards (2025); 10% trans PwDs covered; 2025: TG-specific rehab guidelines.

- Structure: Police-led; 700+ districts. 		700+ cells (2025); 2k cases resolved; 2025: MHA advisory for expansion.
District Transgender Welfare Committees Varies (e.g., 2021 UP) District Administrations (under State Boards) - Objective: Local scheme implementation, grievances.
- Scope: IDs, pensions; awareness.
- Application: Grassroots support. 		- Core Functions: Camps, verification.
- Activities: Skill programs.
- Structure: DM-chaired; 700+ districts. 		1k+ camps (2025); 5k IDs via districts; 2025: Digital tracking.

- All policies, acts, welfare schemes, etc in India for health -
Act/Policy/Scheme Year Authority Purpose/Description Key Provisions Recent Statistics/Impact (2023-2025)
Constitutional Provisions
Fundamental Rights (Part III) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Guarantees right to life, liberty, equality, and health as part of dignity under Articles 14, 19, 21.
- Scope: Protects against state denial of healthcare; enables access to medical services.
- Application: Basis for judicial mandates on public health emergencies, free treatment. 		- Provisions: Art. 14 (equality), Art. 21 (life/dignity including health), Art. 19 (right to practice profession).
- Implementation: Enforced via PILs; e.g., Paschim Banga Khet Mazdoor Samity (1996) on emergency care. 		Invoked in 2025 SC rulings on digital health privacy; supports 100% TB coverage under NTEP.
Article 39(e) (Directive Principles) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: Directs state to ensure health of workers, prevent health hazards in occupations.
- Scope: Protects labor health; promotes safe working conditions.
- Application: Guides OSH policies, ESI for industrial workers. 		- Provisions: State to secure health in occupations; non-justiciable but policy directive.
- Implementation: Influences Factories Act, ESI Act. 		Supports 2025 labor health reforms; 50M+ workers under ESI (2025).
Article 41 (Directive Principles) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: State to secure public assistance in cases of unemployment, old age, sickness, disability.
- Scope: Includes health security for vulnerable.
- Application: Basis for NSAP, PMJAY. 		- Provisions: Right to work, education, assistance within economic capacity.
- Implementation: Guides social security schemes. 		2.5 Cr elderly under NSAP (2025); integrated with PMJAY senior coverage.
Article 42 (Directive Principles) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: State to secure just/humane work conditions, maternity relief.
- Scope: Occupational health, maternal care.
- Application: Influences PMMVY, labor health. 		- Provisions: Maternity relief; humane conditions.
- Implementation: Basis for Maternity Benefit Act. 		3 Cr mothers under PMMVY (2025); 26-week leave compliance 70%.
Article 47 (Directive Principles) 1950 Constitution of India - Objective: State to improve public health as primary duty; prohibit intoxicating drinks/drugs.
- Scope: Nutrition, hygiene, public health improvement.
- Application: Guides NHM, POSHAN. 		- Provisions: Raise nutrition levels, standard of living.
- Implementation: Influences NFSA. 		Stunting reduced 7% via POSHAN (2025); 80 Cr NFSA beneficiaries.
Article 48A (Directive Principles) 1976 Constitution of India - Objective: State to protect/improve environment for healthy development.
- Scope: Pollution control, eco-health link.
- Application: Supports NACP, climate-health integration. 		- Provisions: Endeavor to protect environment.
- Implementation: Via NGT, EPA. 		2025: Integrated in NDHM for eco-health data; 10M air pollution-linked cases reduced.
Legislative Acts
Mental Healthcare Act 2017 Central Legislation - Objective: Provides rights-based mental healthcare; decriminalizes suicide attempts.
- Scope: Protects persons with mental illness (PMI); advance directives, nominated reps.
- Application: Aligns with UNCRPD; mandates community integration. 		- Provisions: Right to treatment, confidentiality; MHBs for oversight (Sections 3-92).
- Implementation: Rules 2018; Tele-MANAS linkage. 		1 Cr PMI screened (2025); 5k MHBs established; Tele-MANAS scaled to 50 states.
National Food Security Act (NFSA) 2013 Central Legislation - Objective: Legal entitlement to subsidized foodgrains; nutritional security.
- Scope: 81 Cr beneficiaries; 5 kg/person/month at ₹2-3/kg.
- Application: PDS reform; maternity benefits. 		- Provisions: 75% rural, 50% urban coverage; grievance redressal (Sections 3-12).
- Implementation: State PDS; Aadhaar-linked. 		80 Cr beneficiaries (2025); ₹2.5 lakh Cr savings; 2025: Integrated with POSHAN 2.0.
The Epidemic Diseases (Amendment) Act 2020 Central Legislation - Objective: Criminalizes assault on healthcare workers during epidemics.
- Scope: Protects frontline staff; amends 1897 Act.
- Application: COVID-19 response; future pandemics. 		- Provisions: Up to 7 years imprisonment for assault (Section 3).
- Implementation: IPC linkage; state enforcement. 		500+ cases prosecuted (2023-25); 2025: Extended to cyber threats on health workers.
National Policies
National Health Policy 2017 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Achieve UHC; increase public health expenditure to 2.5% GDP by 2025.
- Scope: Preventive care, primary health; NCD reduction.
- Application: Guides NHM, AB-HWC. 		- Provisions: Targets MMR 100, IMR 28 by 2020; 70% coverage essential services.
- Implementation: Annual reviews; aligned with SDGs. 		2.1% GDP health spend (2025); MMR 97, IMR 23; 80% essential services covered.
Health Insurance Schemes
Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (PMJAY) 2018 National Health Authority - Objective: ₹5 lakh/family/year cashless coverage for 55 Cr poor; UHC component.
- Scope: Secondary/tertiary care; 1,949 procedures.
- Application: Empanelled hospitals; senior citizens 70+ added (2024). 		- Provisions: Family floater; no cap on family size; 70% seniors covered.
- Implementation: ABHA linkage; 37 Cr cards. 		36.9 Cr cards (Mar 2025); 8.5 Cr hospitalizations; ₹1.25 lakh Cr savings; 6 Cr seniors added.
Rashtriya Swasthya Bima Yojana (RSBY) 2008 Ministry of Labour and Employment - Objective: Health insurance for BPL/unorganized workers; ₹30,000/family/year.
- Scope: Hospitalization; portable coverage.
- Application: Precursor to PMJAY; subsumed in many states. 		- Provisions: Cashless; annual premium ₹30/family.
- Implementation: Smart cards; state-specific. 		Subsumed in PMJAY (2025); legacy 3 Cr beneficiaries; 2025: Integrated data in ABDM.
Mental Health Schemes
KIRAN Helpline 2020 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Toll-free mental health support amid COVID; counseling, referrals.
- Scope: 24/7; multilingual.
- Application: Links to Tele-MANAS. 		- Provisions: Helpline 1800-599-0019; NIMHANS-led.
- Implementation: Call centers, psychologists. 		1 Cr calls (2023-25); 2025: AI chatbots added.
Tele-MANAS 2022 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Tele-mental health service; 24/7 support.
- Scope: 13 languages; 50 states.
- Application: Crisis intervention, therapy. 		- Provisions: Helpline 14416; NIMHANS hubs.
- Implementation: 20 Tele-MANAS cells. 		1 Cr calls (2025); 50 states scaled; 2025: 20% trans mental health focus.
MANODARPAN 2020 Ministry of Education - Objective: Mental health for students; web portal, helpline.
- Scope: School/college counseling.
- Application: COVID stress management. 		- Provisions: Helpline 8448-44-2020; online resources.
- Implementation: NCERT-led. 		5 lakh students supported (2025); 2025: AI tools integrated.
Digital Health Initiatives
Ayushman Bharat Digital Mission (ABDM) 2020 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Digital health ecosystem; ABHA creation, interoperability.
- Scope: Registries for health IDs, facilities, professionals.
- Application: Links PMJAY, e-Sanjeevani. 		- Provisions: ABHA (74 Cr created); HFR, HPR.
- Implementation: NHA; ₹1,600 Cr (2021-26). 		74 Cr ABHA (Feb 2025); 3.6 lakh facilities, 5.6 lakh professionals; 49 Cr records linked.
Aarogya-eSanjeevani 2020 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Telemedicine platform; OPD consultations.
- Scope: Hub-spoke model; rural access.
- Application: COVID response; general care. 		- Provisions: Video/audio consultations; e-prescriptions.
- Implementation: ABDM-integrated. 		3 Cr consultations (2025); 1.5 lakh doctors; 2025: 50 states covered.
COWIN 2021 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: COVID vaccination management; digital certificates.
- Scope: Registration, tracking, certificates.
- Application: Expanded to routine immunizations. 		- Provisions: QR codes; ABHA linkage.
- Implementation: 220 Cr doses. 		220 Cr doses (2025); 2025: Routine vaccine integration.
eHospital 2015 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Hospital management system; patient records, OPD/IPD.
- Scope: Govt hospitals; digital workflows.
- Application: Reduces paperwork. 		- Provisions: EMR, billing; ABDM-linked.
- Implementation: 2,000+ hospitals. 		2k hospitals (2025); 10 Cr records digitized.
eBloodbank 2006 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Online blood donation management; donor registry.
- Scope: Blood availability; safe transfusion.
- Application: Reduces shortages. 		- Provisions: Real-time inventory; ABDM integration.
- Implementation: State networks. 		5k camps (2025); 1 Cr units transfused.
ORS (Oral Rehydration Solution) Programme 1975 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Prevent dehydration in diarrhea; child health focus.
- Scope: Free ORS packets; community distribution.
- Application: Reduces U5 mortality. 		- Provisions: Zinc co-administration; ASHAs.
- Implementation: NHM-integrated. 		80% coverage (2025); 1 Cr packets/year; IMR down 20%.
Primary Care Initiatives
Ayushman Bharat – Health and Wellness Centres (HWCs) 2018 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Strengthen primary care; preventive/promotive health.
- Scope: 1.5 lakh sub-centres upgraded; NCD screening.
- Application: AB-HWC component of NHM. 		- Provisions: 12 services; yoga, NCD care.
- Implementation: ASHAs, ANMs. 		1.76 lakh HWCs (Apr 2025); 107 Cr hypertension, 94 Cr diabetes screenings.
National Health Mission (NHM) 2013 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: RMNCH+A; UHC via primary/secondary care.
- Scope: NRHM+NUHM; JSY, JSSK.
- Application: State-specific. 		- Provisions: Free drugs, diagnostics; 60:40 funding.
- Implementation: State missions. 		₹37,500 Cr (2024-25); MMR 97, IMR 23 (2025).
POSHAN Abhiyaan 2018 Ministry of Women and Child Development - Objective: Reduce malnutrition, stunting, anemia in children, women.
- Scope: 0-6 years, pregnant/lactating mothers; ICDS-based.
- Application: Aligns with NFSA, SDG 2. 		- Provisions: Nutrition tracking; ₹9,000 Cr budget.
- Implementation: Anganwadis, ASHA workers. 		7% stunting reduction (2025); 10 Cr children covered; 2025: Digital tracking scaled.
Janani Suraksha Yojana (JSY) 2005 Ministry of Health and Family Welfare - Objective: Promote institutional deliveries; reduce MMR, NMR.
- Scope: BPL, SC/ST mothers; cash incentives.
- Application: NHM component. 		- Provisions: ₹1,400-₹2,000 per delivery.
- Implementation: ASHA-led. 		1.5 Cr deliveries (2025); MMR down to 97.
Pradhan Mantri Matru Vandana Yojana (PMMVY) 2017 Ministry of Women and Child Development - Objective: Financial support for pregnant/lactating mothers.
- Scope: ₹5,000-₹6,000 for first child.
- Application: Reduces maternal mortality. 		- Provisions: DBT; maternity benefits.
- Implementation: Anganwadis. 		3 Cr mothers (2025); 70% compliance for 26-week leave.
State-Level Schemes
Chief Minister's Comprehensive Health Insurance Scheme (Tamil Nadu) 2009 Government of Tamil Nadu - Objective: Universal coverage; ₹5 lakh/family/year.
- Scope: All residents; 1,000+ procedures.
- Application: Complements PMJAY. 		- Provisions: Cashless; United India Insurance.
- Implementation: State health dept. 		1.5 Cr families (2025); 10 lakh claims/year.

- Scope: 2,400 procedures.

- Scope: Secondary/tertiary care.

Mahatma Jyotiba Phule Jan Arogya Yojana (Maharashtra) 2012 Government of Maharashtra - Objective: ₹1.5 lakh/family/year for BPL; critical care.
- Scope: 971 procedures; rural/urban.
- Application: State insurance complementing PMJAY. 		- Provisions: Cashless; empanelled hospitals.

Biju Swasthya Kalyan Yojana (Odisha) 2018 Government of Odisha - Objective: ₹5 lakh/family/year for 70% population; women up to ₹10 lakh.
